⚠️ *M5 is schematic: coastline shape, spacing and positions are indicative and not to projection. **No regional reef area appears on it**, in compliance with U-04. The world distribution is **M1**, Lesson 5.*

### 1. The intuition, before any terminology

⚠️ Lesson 10 showed that India cannot say how much reef it has without saying which question it is answering. ❗ **This lesson shows something sharper: India cannot say what happened to those reefs without contradicting itself.**

⚠️ One ministry **measured** bleaching and published percentages. ⚠️ Another ministry — the one that owns environment policy — published a **characterisation** saying bleaching in India is sporadic and economically unimportant. ⚠️ Then the **same** ministry that published the characterisation published a measurement of **about 98 per cent** on three named islands, and then **fourteen months later** published the characterisation again.

❗ **The examiner's question is not "who is right".** ❗ The question is: **what kind of statement is each one, and what follows from the fact that both are official?** ⚠️ Answering that is worth more than picking a side, and picking a side is not available on the evidence.

### 2. The measured record, in full

📰 **PIB Release ID 2080596, Ministry of Earth Sciences, posted 04 DEC 2024**, tabulates **Coral Bleaching Status 2023-24**:

| 📰 Region | 📰 Bleaching 2023-24 | 📰 Qualitative description | 📰 Post-bleaching status | 📰 Cause given |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 📰 **Lakshadweep** | **84.6 per cent** | *"widespread bleaching"* | *"some of the reefs recovered"* | *"Increase in sea surface temperature: **El Niño effect**"* |
| 📰 **Gulf of Mannar** | **27 per cent** | *"small scale bleaching in some patchy areas"* | *"most of the reefs recovered"* | Same |
| 📰 **Andaman and Nicobar** | **15-18 per cent**, *"in South Andaman Islands only"* | *"small scale bleaching in the Andaman region only especially South Andaman region"* | *"most of the reefs recovered"* | Same |

❗ ⚠️ **A gap inside the source itself, disclosed (U-19).** ⚠️ The release's prose names **four** affected regions but the table carries rows for only **three** — **the Gulf of Kachchh is named in the sentence and has no row**. ❗ **This edition reports the three tabulated rows as the tabulated record and notes the fourth region's absence from the table**, rather than silently presenting three as four or four as three.

📰 **MoEFCC, Lok Sabha Unstarred Question No. 3414, answered 16.12.2024**, sharpens the Lakshadweep figure to island level: *"**ICAR-CMFRI** has reported widespread coral bleaching in Lakshadweep. The Fourth Global Coral Bleaching Event (**GCBE4**) reported to occur during period, beginning of **2023 till mid May 2024**, has affected the coral reef in Lakshadweep. As informed by Department of Environment and Forest, Lakshadweep Administration, three islands namely **Kadmat, Kavaratti and Kiltan** has experienced **about 98 per cent of coral bleaching** during GCBE4."*

⚠️ ❗ **84.6 per cent and about 98 per cent are not rivals.** ⚠️ The first is an **archipelago-level** figure for 2023-24; the second is an **island-level** figure for three named islands over the GCBE4 window. ❗ **The second refines the first**, and an answer that presents them as contradictory has mistaken a change of scale for a disagreement — exactly the error C1's scale control at lines 217-220 exists to prevent.

❗ ⚠️ **And the recovery discipline (U-17), restated because it is the most tempting place to invent a number.** ⚠️ *"Most of the reefs recovered"* and *"some of the reefs recovered"* are the **official words**. ❗ **No recovery percentage exists in any retrieved primary source and none may be constructed.** ⚠️ What the two phrases do license is a **comparison of degree**: recovery was described as greater where bleaching was less severe, which is consistent with the recovery-capacity argument of Lesson 8.

### 3. The characterisation record, in full

📰 **MoEFCC, Lok Sabha Unstarred Question No. 2249, answered 05.08.2024**: bleaching was *"reported during **March 2024**"* in Lakshadweep; during *"**2023, 2022, 2021 and 2020**, the events of coral bleaching were **not significant**"*; and *"Coral bleaching is a **sporadic event** in India, and such incidents **do not have major impact on local economy**."*

📰 **MoEFCC, Lok Sabha Unstarred Question No. 247, answered 02.02.2026**: *"**Very few** major coral bleaching episodes have been reported, particularly in Lakshadweep, the Andaman and Nicobar Islands, and the Gulf of Mannar **between 2015 and 2026**"*, followed by the identical *"sporadic ... no major impact on local economy"* formula.

❗ ⚠️ **A second disclosure about Q.247 (U-18).** ⚠️ Sub-question **(f)** of that question asked for **the number of coral bleaching alerts issued since 2015, State and Union-Territory-wise**. ❗ **The answer supplies no such series.** ❗ **This edition records that the alert series was requested and not supplied, and substitutes no other series in its place.**

⚠️ **Note also the internal divergence on the event window**, which is smaller but real: **Q.2249 of 05.08.2024** dates the Lakshadweep event to **March 2024**, while **Q.3414 of 16.12.2024** dates GCBE4's effect from **the beginning of 2023**. ❗ Both are stated; neither is adjudicated.

### 4. The contradiction, framed rather than resolved (N-24)

⚠️ Set the two records side by side, with their dates:

| ⚠️ Date | ⚠️ Issuing authority | ⚠️ Statement type | ⚠️ Content |
|---|---|---|---|
| 📰 **05.08.2024** | MoEFCC, LS Q.2249 | ⚠️ **Characterisation** | *"sporadic event ... do not have major impact on local economy"*; 2020-2023 *"not significant"* |
| 📰 **04 DEC 2024** | MoES, PIB 2080596 | ⚠️ **Measurement** | Lakshadweep **84.6 per cent**, *"widespread bleaching"* |
| 📰 **16.12.2024** | **MoEFCC**, LS Q.3414 | ⚠️ **Measurement** | **About 98 per cent** on Kadmat, Kavaratti and Kiltan during GCBE4 |
| 📰 **02.02.2026** | **MoEFCC**, LS Q.247 | ⚠️ **Characterisation** | *"**Very few** major ... episodes ... between 2015 and 2026"*; the same *"sporadic ... no major impact"* formula |

❗ **Three things are true simultaneously and all three must be said.**

1. ❗ **Both sides are official, dated and attributable.** ⚠️ Neither is a press report, a secondary summary or an advocacy claim.
2. ❗ **The contradiction is intra-ministerial, not merely inter-ministerial.** ⚠️ MoEFCC reported **about 98 per cent** on three islands in **December 2024** and wrote *"very few major ... episodes"* and *"sporadic"* in **February 2026** — **fourteen months apart**.
3. ❗ **This edition does not adjudicate**, because no evidence available here permits adjudication. ⚠️ Neither figure is withdrawn in the record; neither answer references the other.

⚠️ **One observation is permitted and is not an adjudication:** **one of the two is a measurement and the other is a characterisation.** ⚠️ A measurement states a proportion of colonies or reef area affected during a defined window. ⚠️ A characterisation states a judgement about **frequency** (*"sporadic"*, *"very few"*) and **consequence** (*"no major impact on local economy"*). ❗ **They are different kinds of claim, and a measurement does not automatically refute a characterisation about frequency or economic impact** — which is precisely why the pair is interesting rather than merely embarrassing.

❗ **What an answer should do with it.** ⚠️ Quote both. ⚠️ Date both. ⚠️ Attribute both. ⚠️ Observe the measurement-versus-characterisation distinction. ⚠️ Note that the economic-impact claim is **unsupported by any published series in the retrieved record**, and that the alert series requested in Q.247(f) was **not supplied**. ❗ **Then stop.** ⚠️ **Choosing a side converts a defensible analysis into an undefended assertion.**

### 5. The reef map of India is not closed (N-23)

📰 **MoEFCC, LS Q.4311, 13.12.2019**, names **four** *"major reef formation areas"*: **Gulf of Kutch, Gulf of Mannar, Lakshadweep Islands, Andaman and Nicobar Islands**.

📰 **PIB 2080596, MoES, 04 DEC 2024**, adds **Malvan, Maharashtra** as a newly mapped reef and names **Palk Bay** as an **NCCR** monitoring site. 📰 The same release records that SAC's preliminary results indicate *"apparent loss of reef area for **Malvan** reef in Maharashtra over a period of **thirteen years (2007 to 2020)**"*, and the same for **four of the eleven reefs mapped for the Gulf of Mannar** — **Koswari, Nalla Tanni Tivu, Pichaimoopan Valasai and Shingle reef** — over **twelve to fourteen years, 2004/05 to 2018**.

❗ **Therefore "India has four coral reef areas" is a dated statement, not a permanent one.** ⚠️ The correct forms are either *"the four **major** reef formation areas named by MoEFCC on 13.12.2019"* or *"four major areas named in 2019, with Malvan mapped and Palk Bay monitored as recorded on 04 DEC 2024"*. ❗ **A bare "four" with no date is wrong by 2024.**

⚠️ ❗ **And a discipline on the decadal results.** ⚠️ The release's own word is *"apparent"*. ❗ **This edition reproduces "apparent loss of reef area" and does not upgrade it to "loss", and states no magnitude, because none is given.**

### 6. The monitoring architecture, as a geography of observation

⚠️ ❗ **This block is carried as a named inventory, not narrated**, because the institutions are a **geography of observation** while their programmes are not Geography's to explain.

| 📰 Institution | 📰 Parent | 📰 What it runs | 📰 Source and date |
|---|---|---|---|
| 📰 **NCSCM** | MoEFCC | **CReON** — the Coral Reef in situ Observation Network — for long-term reef health, **calcification rates and ocean acidification**, using data buoys and automated weather stations | PIB 2080596, **04 DEC 2024** |
| 📰 **SAC, ISRO** | Department of Space | **Inventory of Indian Coral Reefs**, at the behest of DBT and DOS; earlier built the **Coral Bleaching Monitoring and Alert System** hosted on the **VEDAS** geo-portal | PIB 2080596, **04 DEC 2024**; VEDAS also in LS Q.4311, **13.12.2019** |
| 📰 **INCOIS** | MoES | **Coral Bleaching Alert System (CBAS)** and **Marine Heatwave Alerts** | PIB 2080596, **04 DEC 2024**; LS Q.247, **02.02.2026** |
| 📰 **NCCR** | MoES | Monitoring of **Palk Bay, Andaman and Lakshadweep** | PIB 2080596, **04 DEC 2024** |
| 📰 **ZSI** | MoEFCC | **Long Term Coral Reef Monitoring Program**; work on bleaching-resistant corals and zooxanthellae; the **143.46 sq km** mapping of Lesson 10 | PIB 2080596, **04 DEC 2024** |
| 📰 **CSIR-NIO** | CSIR | **C-Bot**, the Coral Reef Monitoring Robot, with underwater imaging and geo-referencing *"vital for continuous monitoring and **validation of satellite data**"* | PIB 2080596, **04 DEC 2024**; LS Q.247, **02.02.2026** |
| 📰 **ICAR-CMFRI** | ICAR | A national coral-reef **resilience** project; reported the Lakshadweep bleaching | LS Q.247, **02.02.2026**; LS Q.3414, **16.12.2024** |
| 📰 **National Coastal Mission project** | MoEFCC | *"Development of Corals and its Management"* | LS Q.247, **02.02.2026** |

❗ **Why this is geography and not institutional listing.** ⚠️ Read as a system, the inventory is a **ground-truthing chain**: a satellite detects a thermal anomaly and issues an alert; a buoy records the in-situ temperature and chemistry; a robot images the reef and geo-references the image; a diver survey counts bleached colonies. ⚠️ **Each link exists because the previous one cannot be trusted alone**, and the release says so of C-Bot in terms — it is *"vital for ... validation of satellite data"*. ❗ **That is a distributed marine observation network described by what each node can and cannot see**, which is a spatial argument about the production of knowledge, and it is the same argument Lesson 10 made about mapping scale.

📰 ⚠️ Two further dated items belong with the inventory and nowhere else: **corals are listed under Schedule-I** of the Wild Life (Protection) Act (LS Q.247, **02.02.2026**) — ❗ stated once **as a legal fact**, with the **listing rationale routed out**; and **16,000 corals were translocated in the Gulf of Kachchh** (PIB 2080596, **04 DEC 2024**) — ❗ stated once as a dated official figure, with **restoration technique routed out**.

### 7. 🇮🇳 India hook — the whole lesson, and the routing it must perform

🇮🇳 ⚠️ This lesson is entirely Indian. ❗ Its India hook is the **sentence pair** it makes available for the owned Mains demand, which needs **dated official Indian examples** and has only these:

> ⚠️ *"India's own record shows both the impact and the difficulty of assessing it. The Ministry of Earth Sciences recorded Lakshadweep at **84.6 per cent** bleaching in 2023-24 and the Gulf of Mannar at **27 per cent** (**04 DEC 2024**), while the Ministry of Environment reported **about 98 per cent** bleaching on Kadmat, Kavaratti and Kiltan during the Fourth Global Coral Bleaching Event, dated from the beginning of 2023 to mid-May 2024 (**16.12.2024**) — yet the same ministry described Indian bleaching as a **'sporadic event'** with **'no major impact on local economy'** on **05.08.2024** and again on **02.02.2026**."*

🔁 ❗ **The routing this lesson must perform, and it is the one most likely to be got wrong.** ⚠️ **2022 Prelims Question 49 asks about biorock technology in coral reef restoration.** ⚠️ It is the **closest objective question to this topic in the entire ledger set**. ❗ **It is owned by `Environment-and-Ecology/basic/24_Coastal-and-Marine-Ecology-CRZ-Blue-Economy`, not by Topic 11.** ⚠️ **Restoration technique is not island or reef geomorphology.** ❗ **Topic 11 teaches the reef mechanics that make restoration intelligible and names the owner at the point of temptation; it states no option, no key and no elimination for that demand anywhere in this file.** 🔁 **Marine pollution, dead zones, blue carbon and the Deep Ocean Mission route to the same owner.**

### 8. Comparison, objection and the residual

| | ⚠️ The measurement record | ⚠️ The characterisation record |
|---|---|---|
| ⚠️ Issuing authority | 📰 MoES (04 DEC 2024) and **MoEFCC** (16.12.2024) | 📰 **MoEFCC** (05.08.2024 and 02.02.2026) |
| ⚠️ Form of claim | ⚠️ Proportions, for named regions and islands, in a defined window | ⚠️ Judgements of **frequency** and **economic consequence** |
| ⚠️ Supporting series published? | ⚠️ Yes — a table with regions, values and causes | ❗ **No.** The alert series requested in Q.247(f) **was not supplied (U-18)** |
| ⚠️ Can it be checked? | ⚠️ In principle, against the monitoring architecture above | ⚠️ Not from the retrieved record |
| ⚠️ What this edition does | ❗ Quotes with date and authority | ❗ Quotes with date and authority |

**Strongest objection.** ⚠️ Refusing to adjudicate is evasion. A candidate who presents both sides and declines to conclude has written a balanced non-answer, and examiners penalise fence-sitting.

**Reply.** ⚠️ The objection confuses **declining to adjudicate a factual contradiction between two official sources** with **declining to conclude**. ❗ This edition concludes a great deal: that the two statements are of **different logical types**; that the characterisation's economic claim is **unsupported by any published series in the retrieved record**; that the series specifically requested by Parliament in Q.247(f) was **not supplied**; and that the measured record is **internally coherent across scales** once archipelago and island levels are distinguished. ⚠️ What it declines is to assert, without evidence, that one official document is false. ❗ **That is not fence-sitting; it is the difference between an evidenced conclusion and an invented one**, and it is exactly what an *assess* directive rewards.

❓ **Residual, which this edition does not close.** ⚠️ **No reconciliation of the two records exists in the retrieved sources**, and neither answer acknowledges the other. ⚠️ ❓ **No bleaching series for India after the February 2026 answer was retrieved**, so the record as carried here ends on **02.02.2026** and this edition makes **no claim about any later position**.

### 9. Traps specific to this lesson

- ❌ ⚠️ **Treating 84.6 per cent and about 98 per cent as contradictory.** ❗ Archipelago-level versus island-level; the second **refines** the first.
- ❌ ⚠️ **Adjudicating the ministry contradiction.** ❗ Quote both, date both, attribute both, observe the measurement-versus-characterisation distinction, and stop.
- ❌ ⚠️ **Converting "most of the reefs recovered" into a percentage.** ❗ **No recovery figure exists (U-17).**
- ❌ ⚠️ **Writing "India has four coral reef areas" without a date.** ❗ Four **major** areas as of **13.12.2019**; Malvan mapped and Palk Bay monitored as of **04 DEC 2024** (N-23).
- ❌ ⚠️ **Upgrading "apparent loss of reef area" to "loss".** ❗ The release's own word is *"apparent"*, and no magnitude is given.
- ❌ ⚠️ **Repeating the "~117 coral species" or "first marine biosphere reserve" claim.** ❗ **Not verified in primary form this audit (U-07)**; designation routes to `Environment-and-Ecology/basic/07_Biosphere-Reserves-and-Ramsar-Sites`.
- ❌ ⚠️ **Explaining biorock, transplantation or restoration technique.** 🔁 **2022 Prelims Q49 is Environment's**, and the technique is not reef geomorphology.
- ❌ ⚠️ **Stating a regional reef area to accompany a bleaching percentage.** ❗ **No regional reef area appears anywhere in this edition (U-04).**

### UPSC application

- **Syllabus:** ⚠️ Mapped clause, **second and third limbs** — *changes in critical geographical features* and *"the effects of such changes"*. ⚠️ The bleaching record is the change; the recovery language and the economic characterisation are claims about the effects.
- **Verified PYQ:** ❗ **This lesson supplies the examples half of O2 — 2019 GS-I Q4, *"Global warming impact on the coral life system"*, *Assess with examples*, 10 marks / 150 words.** ⚠️ **These are the only dated, official, Indian examples available**, which is why they are decisive. 🔁 ❗ **2022 Prelims Q49, biorock, is routed to `Environment-and-Ecology/basic/24`; no key, option or elimination appears for it anywhere in this file.**
- **Traps:** ⚠️ The eight above. ❗ Adjudicating the contradiction and inventing a recovery percentage are the two that an examiner can detect immediately.
- **Answer use:** ❗ In a 150-word budget you can afford **two dated examples and one framing clause**, and no more. ⚠️ *"Lakshadweep recorded 84.6 per cent bleaching in 2023-24 (MoES, 04 DEC 2024) and three of its islands about 98 per cent during the Fourth Global Coral Bleaching Event (MoEFCC, 16.12.2024), although the same ministry has described Indian bleaching as sporadic with no major economic impact (05.08.2024 and 02.02.2026) — a measurement set against a characterisation."* ⚠️ That is about fifty-five words and it carries the whole finding.

Revision notes:

- 📰 **Measured record (PIB 2080596, MoES, 04 DEC 2024):** **Lakshadweep 84.6 per cent**, *"widespread bleaching"*, *"some of the reefs recovered"* · **Gulf of Mannar 27 per cent**, *"small scale ... patchy"*, *"most ... recovered"* · **A&N 15-18 per cent, South Andaman only**, *"most ... recovered"*. ⚠️ Cause in every row: **SST rise, El Niño**.
- ❗ **[U-19] The release's prose names four regions; its table carries three.** ❗ **Gulf of Kachchh is named and has no row.** Reported as such.
- 📰 **Island-level (MoEFCC LS Q.3414, 16.12.2024):** **Kadmat, Kavaratti, Kiltan** *"about **98 per cent** of coral bleaching"* during **GCBE4**, dated *"beginning of **2023 till mid May 2024**"*; reported by **ICAR-CMFRI** and the Lakshadweep Administration.
- ❗ **84.6 and ~98 are NOT rivals** — archipelago versus island scale; the second **refines** the first.
- 📰 **Characterisation (MoEFCC LS Q.2249, 05.08.2024):** event *"during **March 2024**"*; 2020-2023 *"**not significant**"*; *"Coral bleaching is a **sporadic event** in India, and such incidents **do not have major impact on local economy**."* 📰 **Restated (LS Q.247, 02.02.2026):** *"**Very few** major coral bleaching episodes ... **between 2015 and 2026**"* plus the identical formula.
- ❗ **[N-24 / G14] The contradiction is INTRA-ministerial and fourteen months wide.** ❗ **Quote both, date both, attribute both, do not adjudicate.** ⚠️ Permitted observation: **one is a measurement, the other a characterisation.**
- ❗ **[U-18] Q.247 sub-question (f) asked for bleaching alerts since 2015, State-wise. No series was supplied.** ❗ Stated; **no substitute series offered**.
- ⚠️ **Event-window divergence:** **March 2024** (Q.2249) against **beginning of 2023** (Q.3414). Both stated. ❗ **[U-17] "Most/some of the reefs recovered" is qualitative; no recovery percentage exists.**
- ❌ **[N-23] "India has four coral reef areas" is DATED.** 📰 Four **major** areas, **13.12.2019**: Gulf of Kutch, Gulf of Mannar, Lakshadweep, A&N. 📰 **Malvan** mapped and **Palk Bay** monitored, **04 DEC 2024**.
- 📰 **Decadal results, with the release's own hedge:** *"**apparent** loss of reef area"* for **Malvan, 2007 to 2020 (thirteen years)**, and for **four of eleven** Gulf of Mannar reefs — **Koswari, Nalla Tanni Tivu, Pichaimoopan Valasai, Shingle** — over **2004/05 to 2018**. ❗ No magnitude given; *"apparent"* retained.
- 📰 **Monitoring architecture, as a ground-truthing chain:** **NCSCM/CReON** · **SAC ISRO** inventory and **VEDAS** alert system · **INCOIS CBAS** and marine heatwave alerts · **NCCR** (Palk Bay, Andaman, Lakshadweep) · **ZSI LTCRMP** · **CSIR-NIO C-Bot**, *"vital for ... validation of satellite data"* · **ICAR-CMFRI** resilience project · NCM project *"Development of Corals and its Management"*.
- 📰 **Stated once as dated facts, then routed:** corals under **Schedule-I** (LS Q.247, 02.02.2026); **16,000 corals translocated** in the Gulf of Kachchh (PIB 2080596, 04 DEC 2024).
- ❗ **[U-07]** C1 line 72's *"first marine biosphere reserve, ~117 coral species"* is **flagged, not repeated**. 🔁 `Environment-and-Ecology/basic/07`.
- 🔁 **2022 Prelims Q49 (biorock) → `Environment-and-Ecology/basic/24`.** 🔁 Marine pollution, dead zones, blue carbon, Deep Ocean Mission → same owner.
- 🔑 **Mnemonic — "Measured above the line, characterised below it."**

### Concept check

**Question:** An examiner asks you to assess the official Indian evidence on coral bleaching. Set out what the evidence establishes, what it leaves unresolved, and what a candidate must not do with it.

**Model answer:** The evidence falls into two kinds and the distinction is the analysis. **Measurements**: the Ministry of Earth Sciences recorded Coral Bleaching Status 2023-24 as Lakshadweep 84.6 per cent with *"widespread bleaching"*, Gulf of Mannar 27 per cent and Andaman and Nicobar 15-18 per cent *"in South Andaman Islands only"*, attributing every row to sea-surface temperature rise and the El Niño effect, in a release posted 04 DEC 2024; and the Ministry of Environment, Forest and Climate Change told the Lok Sabha on 16.12.2024 that Kadmat, Kavaratti and Kiltan experienced *"about 98 per cent of coral bleaching"* during the Fourth Global Coral Bleaching Event, dated *"beginning of 2023 till mid May 2024"*. These are consistent once scale is observed: the island-level figure refines the archipelago-level one rather than contradicting it. **Characterisations**: the same environment ministry wrote on 05.08.2024, and again on 02.02.2026, that *"coral bleaching is a sporadic event in India, and such incidents do not have major impact on local economy"*, the later answer adding that *"very few"* major episodes occurred between 2015 and 2026. What the evidence **establishes** is that severe bleaching was measured and officially recorded in 2023-24, that it was most severe in Lakshadweep, and that recovery was described qualitatively as greater where bleaching was milder. What it **leaves unresolved** is the relationship between the measured record and the ministry's own characterisation, which no retrieved document reconciles; the economic-impact claim is also unsupported by any published series, and the State-wise alert series Parliament requested in February 2026 was not supplied. What a candidate must **not** do is adjudicate between two dated official statements without evidence, convert *"most of the reefs recovered"* into a figure, present the archipelago and island percentages as rival claims, or attach a regional reef area to any of these percentages, since no first-party regional breakdown is available.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not assume that an official characterisation and an official measurement must be reconcilable, or that the more recent one supersedes the earlier. They are different kinds of claim issued for different purposes, and in this record the later document is the characterisation while the measurement stands unwithdrawn. Treating recency as authority would discard the measured evidence entirely.

**Original Mains practice [20 marks]:** Assess the official Indian record on coral bleaching since 2019, with attention to what the record measures and what it asserts. *(Assess — answer in about 250 words.)*

**Model (about 250 words):** The record divides into measurements and characterisations, and the division is the finding. On the measured side, the Ministry of Earth Sciences published Coral Bleaching Status 2023-24 on 04 DEC 2024: Lakshadweep 84.6 per cent with *"widespread bleaching"*, Gulf of Mannar 27 per cent, and Andaman and Nicobar 15-18 per cent confined to South Andaman, each attributed to sea-surface temperature rise and El Niño, with post-bleaching recovery described only qualitatively. The environment ministry told the Lok Sabha on 16.12.2024 that Kadmat, Kavaratti and Kiltan experienced *"about 98 per cent"* bleaching during the Fourth Global Coral Bleaching Event, dated from early 2023 to mid-May 2024. The island figure refines the archipelago figure once scale is observed. On the asserted side, the same ministry wrote on 05.08.2024, and repeated on 02.02.2026, that bleaching is *"a sporadic event in India"* with *"no major impact on local economy"*, and that *"very few"* major episodes occurred between 2015 and 2026. The two cannot be reconciled from the record, and neither is withdrawn. The honest assessment notes that one is a measurement of proportion in a defined window and the other a judgement about frequency and consequence; that no published series supports the economic claim; and that the State-wise alert series sought in February 2026 was not supplied. Separately, the reef map itself is open: four major areas were named in 2019, with Malvan mapped and Palk Bay monitored by 2024, and four of eleven Gulf of Mannar reefs show *"apparent"* area loss.

**Rubric (quantified, 20 marks):** **6** for the measured record given completely and accurately, with all three 2023-24 regional values, the El Niño attribution, the island-level refinement and the correct scale relationship between 84.6 and about 98 per cent; **5** for the characterisation record given with both dates and the exact official formula, including the *"very few ... between 2015 and 2026"* wording; **5** for dated contradiction framing — both sides quoted, dated and attributed, the intra-ministerial nature of the divergence noted, the measurement-versus-characterisation distinction drawn, and **no adjudication attempted**; **3** for the monitoring architecture used as a ground-truthing chain rather than listed, or for the open-reef-map point with its dated additions and the retained word *"apparent"*; **1** for routing discipline, naming Environment as the owner of restoration technique. Deduct 3 for inventing any recovery percentage; deduct 2 for stating a regional reef area.

⚠️ *M3 is schematic. Island shapes, spacing and positions are indicative and not to projection; it is drawn to carry the arc sequence, the channel order and the lithological section. Lakshadweep is **M4**, Lesson 14; reef regions are **M5**, Lesson 11.*

### 1. The intuition, before any terminology

⚠️ Lesson 1 claimed that the Bay of Bengal chain is a **festoon** — a looped archipelago continuing a mountain range traceable on the continent. ❗ **This lesson proves the claim and then cashes it**, because once the arc is established, four otherwise unrelated facts become a single explanation.

⚠️ **Why the islands are elongated north-south**: because the fold belt is. ⚠️ **Why they are made of sandstone, limestone and shale**: because a fold belt is built of sedimentary rock, not of coral. ⚠️ **Why there is a 737-metre peak in an archipelago whose Arabian Sea counterpart rises five metres**: because this is a mountain range with its feet in the sea. ⚠️ **Why the region is seismically exposed**: because the arc is the northern continuation of a subduction margin.

❗ **One classification, four consequences.** ⚠️ That is what makes the arc worth a lesson rather than a sentence.

### 2. The arc, established from four independent sources

| ⚠️ Source | ✅ What it says |
|---|---|
| ✅ **C2 §1 and §3.1** | The Andamans are *"linked with the **Arakan Yoma** fold chain"* and are *"an extension of the **Tertiary Arakan Yoma**"* fold chain |
| ✅ **Majid, *Geography of India*, printed 2.42** | *"The Andaman Islands have been formed by the **extension of the Tertiary mountain chain of Arakanyoma**. The main rocks of these islands are sandstone, limestone and shale."* |
| ✅ **Khullar, printed folio 89** | *"Most of these islands are made of **tertiary sandstone, limestone and shale resting on basic and ultrabasic volcanoes**."* |
| ✅ **Map bank, certified row** | *"Andaman-Nicobar arc is its [the Sunda Trench's] **northern continuation**."* |

❗ **Four sources, two of them books and two of them repository artefacts, converge on one structural interpretation.** ⚠️ That is the strongest evidential position in the India half of this edition, and it should be stated as convergence rather than asserted as fact.

❌ ❗ **The one dissenting sentence, recorded as a source defect (N-12).** ✅ Majid, *Geography of India*, printed 2.42, states that *"the physiography of the **Nicobar islands** is **mainly of coral origin**"*. ⚠️ That is contradicted by ✅ C2 line 21, which places the Nicobars as *"emergent parts of the same tectonic island arc, with extensive fringing reefs and sedimentary/volcanic rocks"*; by ✅ Khullar's lithology, which has the islands *"fringed"* with coral rather than built of it; and by the map bank's own trap row, *"**Great Nicobar is not a coral atoll**"*. ❗ **This edition teaches the arc interpretation and records the coral-origin sentence as a source defect, not as an alternative view.** ⚠️ The distinction matters: a reef **on** an island is not the same thing as an island **made of** reef, and Lesson 6's scale discipline is exactly what keeps them apart.

### 3. Relief, lithology and the elevation discipline

✅ **Khullar, printed folio 89**: the highest point of the archipelago is **Saddle Peak**, in **North Andaman**, at **737 m**.

❗ ⚠️ **The attribution rule applied here (N-28, U-05).** ✅ C2 line 25 itself instructs the reader to *"use Survey of India data for exact elevation"*. ❗ **No Survey of India figure was retrieved in this audit.** ❗ Therefore **737 m is attributed to Khullar by name**, this edition **states that the Survey of India value was not verified here**, and **no competing elevation is stated**.

⚠️ **Why the relief fact matters beyond being a fact.** ⚠️ A peak of this order on an island chain is **only possible on a structurally continental island**. ⚠️ A coral island cannot produce it, for the mechanical reason given at Lesson 2; a young volcanic cone can, but then the rock would be volcanic. ❗ **The elevation is therefore corroborating evidence for the arc interpretation**, and using it that way is better than listing it.

⚠️ **Dimensions, all book-era and attributed**, from ✅ **Khullar, printed folios 88-89**: the archipelago extends **6° 45′ N to 13° 45′ N** and **92° 10′ E to 94° 15′ E**, about **590 km** long with a maximum width of about **58 km**; the **Andaman group** is **260 km long and 30 km wide**; the **Nicobar group** is **262 km** long with a maximum width of **58 km**.

❗ ⚠️ **A transcription flag (N-33).** ⚠️ **The same 58 km is given as the maximum width of the whole archipelago and of the Nicobar group alone.** ❗ That is possible but suspicious, and this edition **flags the repeated value as a possible transcription artefact and cites it only with that caveat**.

❗ ⚠️ **An official typographic defect, disclosed and neither reproduced nor silently corrected (N-32).** 📰 The Andaman and Nicobar Administration's About page, **retrieved 04-10-2026**, renders the Union Territory's latitude as *"**60 to 140 North**"*. ⚠️ That is a **typographic mangling of 6° to 14° North**, as the longitude range of **92° to 94° E** on the same page confirms. ❗ **This edition reads it as 6° to 14° N, states that the page's printed form is defective, and does not reproduce the defective string as if it were data.**

### 4. The volcanic outliers, with C2's contradiction resolved and the volcano routed

⚠️ ✅ C2 §2 and §3.1 place **Barren Island** and **Narcondam** east of the Andamans.

❌ ❗ **The resolution of C2's internal contradiction (N-16), stated once more because this is the lesson where a candidate will reach for it.** ✅ C2 line 23 warns against *"treating activity status as settled in a simple active/dormant binary"*; ✅ lines 31 and 56 then assert that Narcondam is *"dormant"*; ✅ line 22 calls Barren *"India's only active volcano"* while line 47 calls it *"India's only **historically** active volcano"*. ❗ **This edition adopts the map bank's hedged formulation: Barren Island is India's only *confirmed* active volcano, and Narcondam is *not treated as currently active*.** ❗ **The Advanced owner's internal inconsistency is stated rather than smoothed over.**

❌ ❗ **A second conflict, taught rather than resolved (N-17).** ✅ **Khullar, printed folio 89**, places the volcanic islands *"**north of Port Blair**"*. ✅ **Majid, printed 2.42**, places them *"within **80 km east** of the Andaman Islands"* and adds that *"The Barren Island in the Andamans **has an active volcano**"*. ⚠️ The map bank's independent formulation — Barren *"**East of Middle Andaman**"*, Narcondam *"**North of Barren Island**"* — **reconciles both descriptions** as a north-east position relative to the capital and an east position relative to the chain. ❗ **This edition teaches the conflict and its reconciliation and asserts no distance**, since the 80 km figure is one book's and was not corroborated.

🔁 ❗ **The route-out this lesson must perform, and it is the single most likely mis-claim in the topic.** ⚠️ **Barren Island's volcanic mechanics, its eruption chronology and its activity classification belong to `Geography/basic/03_Vulcanism-and-Earthquakes`.** ❗ **The 2018 Prelims Question 85 demand — Barren Island active volcano location and eruption history — is owned by Topic 03, not by Topic 11.** ⚠️ Topic 11 supplies the island **as a location and as an island type**; it supplies **nothing about the volcano**. ❗ **No key, option or elimination appears for that demand anywhere in this file.**

### 5. The channel system, which is the chain's map spine

⚠️ **Four channels, each with a discriminator, because every one of them is a standing confusion.**

| ⚠️ Channel | ⚠️ What it separates | ❗ The discriminator |
|---|---|---|
| ⚠️ **Coco Channel** | ⚠️ The Coco Islands of Myanmar from **North Andaman** | ❗ The chain's **northern** boundary channel; not an internal divider |
| ⚠️ **Duncan Passage** | ⚠️ **South Andaman** from **Little Andaman**, south of Rutland Island | ❗ **It is not the Andaman-Nicobar divider.** It lies **within** the Andaman group |
| ⚠️ **Ten Degree Channel** | ⚠️ **Little Andaman** from **Car Nicobar** — the **Andaman group from the Nicobar group** | ❗ **This is the divider**, at about **10° N**. 📰 The A&N Administration, retrieved **04-10-2026**, gives it as *"**some 150 km wide**"* |
| ⚠️ **Sombrero Channel** and **Grand Channel** | ⚠️ Internal Nicobar separations | ❗ Internal only |
| ⚠️ **Great Channel** | ⚠️ **Great Nicobar** from **Sumatra** | ❗ **It is not the Ten Degree Channel.** ✅ Khullar places Great Nicobar **147 km from Sumatra** |

⚠️ ❗ **And the southernmost-point discipline.** ⚠️ **Indira Point**, on Great Nicobar close to the Great Channel, is the **southernmost point of Indian territory**. ❗ **It is not Indira Col**, which is a different feature entirely. ❗ **Kanyakumari is mainland India's southern tip, not India's.** ⚠️ The map bank carries both traps and this edition reproduces them.

### 6. 🇮🇳 India hook — the whole lesson, with exposure stated and hazard routed

🇮🇳 ⚠️ This lesson is entirely Indian. ❗ Its discipline obligation is the strictest in the edition, and it is stated before the content: **Topic 11 has no General Studies Paper III disaster mandate at all.** ⚠️ Neither canonical owner appears in the disaster row of the repository's syllabus-mapping file. ❗ **Seismic, tsunami and cyclone material may appear here only as *exposure statements attached to island geography*, never as hazard or management teaching.**

⚠️ **What this edition therefore says, in full, and then stops:**

- ✅ Majid, printed 2.42: *"The entire region falls in a **major earthquake zone**."*
- ✅ C2 §3.1 agrees: *"The region lies in a major earthquake zone."*
- ✅ The map bank: the **Andaman-Nicobar arc is the northern continuation of the Sunda Trench**.
- ✅ Majid IWG, local scan PDF p.142 (❗ **folio not read; cited by PDF page only, U-15**): the **Banda Aceh (Sumatra) seismic wave of 26 December 2004** in the **Sunda Trench** *"killed over two lakh people"*, and *"The impact of the tsunami was quite serious in **Andaman and Nicobar Islands** and the Tamil Nadu coast of India"*.
- 📰 PIB Research, **1 May 2026**: Great Nicobar *"lies in a **seismically sensitive and cyclone-prone** region"*.

🔁 ❗ **And then the routing, by exact path.** ⚠️ **Tsunami genesis, earthquake mechanics, wave propagation, warning chains, evacuation and institutional disaster doctrine are not taught here.** 🔁 **Earthquake and volcanic process → `Geography/basic/03_Vulcanism-and-Earthquakes`.** 🔁 **Tsunami as a hazard, and the 2025 GS-I Question 7 demand → `Disaster-Management/basic/06_Tsunami-and-Coastal-Hazard-Management`.** ❗ **That ledger row carries subject column "Geography" with a note that physical-process ownership remains Geography; it is still routed, because within Geography the process owner is Topic 03 and not Topic 11, and the inconsistency is recorded rather than exploited (U-02).**

🔁 ❗ **A second mandatory route-out.** ⚠️ **2026 Prelims Question 33 asks about Andaman and Nicobar climate, monsoon rainfall and seasonal precipitation.** ⚠️ It names **the exact island group this lesson maps**. ❗ **It is owned by `Geography/basic/16_Tropical-Monsoon-and-Marine-Climate`.** ❗ **Island climate is routed; no key, option or elimination appears for it anywhere in this file.**

⚠️ **One short settlement-geography block, which is ours**, kept separate from anything that is not:

- 📰 A&N Administration, retrieved **04-10-2026**: 2011 Census population **3,79,944**; literacy **86.27 per cent**; language shares from the 2001 Census — **Bengali 26 per cent, Hindi 18.23, Tamil 17.68, Telugu 12.81, Malayalam 8.11, Nicobarese 8.05**; and *"**Andaman Creole Hindi** is widely used as a trade language"*.
- ✅ Majid, printed 2.42: *"The Tribal population in the Andaman Islands is **fast dwindling**. Most of its present inhabitants are migrants from Bangladesh, Myanmar, and India and Tamils from Sri Lanka. Some of the well known surviving tribes ... are the **Onges, Jarawas and Sentinelese**."*
- 📰 NIC State Profile, **2021 upload path**: **three districts and nine tehsils**.
- ✅ Majid, printed 2.42: the Andamans are *"a **birdwatcher's paradise with 242 species** recorded"*, and the **Giant Robber Crab** occurs on Great Nicobar and in South Andaman.

❗ ⚠️ **The boundary on that block, stated plainly.** ⚠️ **Settlement origin, language distribution and population geography are ours.** 🔁 **Tribal policy, rights, consent, Particularly Vulnerable Tribal Group criteria and constitutional provision are not** — they route to `Social-Justice/basic/08_Scheduled-Tribes-PVTGs-and-Tribal-Welfare` and `Polity/basic/Scheduled-and-Tribal-Areas`. 🔁 **Species status and protected-area designation route to `Environment-and-Ecology/basic/07` and `basic/24`.** ❌ ❗ **Majid's phrase "Wandoor Marine Biosphere Reserve" is not reproduced (N-30):** the protected area at Wandoor in South Andaman is the **Mahatma Gandhi Marine National Park**, as the map bank records, and the biosphere reserve on Great Nicobar is a separate designation.

❌ ❗ **Finally, the name.** 📰 Per **PIB Release ID 2054647, Ministry of Home Affairs, 13 SEP 2024**, the capital is **Sri Vijaya Puram**. ⚠️ **Both books print Port Blair; the NIC State Profile still prints Port Blair; the Administration's current page uses Sri Vijaya Puram.** ❗ **This edition uses Sri Vijaya Puram throughout and records the change with its date (N-27).** 🔁 The renaming's colonial-legacy, Chola and freedom-struggle framing routes to **Modern Indian History** and **Indian Art and Culture**.

### 7. Comparison, objection and the residual

| | ⚠️ Bay of Bengal system (this lesson) | ⚠️ Arabian Sea system (Lesson 14) |
|---|---|---|
| ⚠️ Genetic class | ⚠️ **Continental — festoon / island arc** | ⚠️ **Oceanic — coral** |
| ⚠️ Structure | ✅ Extension of the **Tertiary Arakan Yoma**; northern continuation of the **Sunda Trench** | ⚠️ Reef platforms; foundation **not verified in this edition** |
| ⚠️ Rock | ✅ *"Tertiary sandstone, limestone and shale resting on basic and ultrabasic volcanoes"* | ⚠️ Biogenic carbonate |
| ⚠️ Relief | ✅ **Saddle Peak, 737 m (Khullar)** | ✅ *"Not more than five metre"* (Khullar) |
| ⚠️ Reefs | ⚠️ **Fringing**, sitting **on** a structure they did not build | ⚠️ **Atoll** at tract scale, fringing on each island's seaward face |
| ⚠️ Volcanoes | ⚠️ **Barren** (only confirmed active) and **Narcondam** (not treated as currently active), east of the arc | ⚠️ None |
| ⚠️ Principal exposure | ⚠️ **Seismic**, on an active margin — 🔁 mechanism routed | ⚠️ **Reef-health and lens**, Lesson 9 |

**Strongest objection.** ⚠️ Separating "exposure" from "hazard" is an artificial distinction. If the edition says the region is in a major earthquake zone and that a tsunami struck it in 2004, it has already entered disaster territory and should simply teach the subject properly.

**Reply.** ⚠️ The distinction is not artificial; it is **the syllabus mapping**. ❗ Neither owner appears in the disaster clause, so the topic has **no mandate** there — a stricter position than Topic 10 enjoyed, which held at least a support-only status. ⚠️ **Exposure is a locational property of an island**: being on an active margin is a fact about where the island is, in the same way that Great Nicobar's distance from a shipping route is. ⚠️ **Hazard is a process-and-response field**: genesis, propagation, warning, preparedness, institutional response. ❗ The test used throughout this edition is whether the sentence could be written **from a map and a structural cross-section alone**. ⚠️ *"These islands sit on the northern continuation of the Sunda Trench"* passes; *"tsunami warning requires a network of bottom-pressure recorders"* does not.

❓ **Residual, which this edition does not close.** ⚠️ **Saddle Peak's Survey of India elevation was not retrieved (U-05)** and only Khullar's figure is stated, attributed. ⚠️ ❓ **The opening of Majid's island section — printed 2.38 to 2.41 — is physically absent from the local scan (U-12)**, so only printed **2.42** is cited and **nothing is implied about what the missing pages contained**. ⚠️ ❓ **Khullar's folio offset drifts across the book (U-13)**, so only folios **read on a render** are cited and **no Khullar folio is computed anywhere in this edition**.

### 8. Traps specific to this lesson

- ❌ ⚠️ **Treating the Nicobars as coral in origin.** ❗ Majid's *"mainly of coral origin"* sentence is a **source defect** (N-12); the arc interpretation has four independent sources and the map bank's trap row says *"Great Nicobar is not a coral atoll"*.
- ❌ ⚠️ **Explaining Barren Island's volcanism.** 🔁 `Geography/basic/03`; **2018 Prelims Q85 is Topic 03's**.
- ❌ ⚠️ **"Narcondam is dormant"** as a settled statement. ❗ Use **"not treated as currently active"**, and note C2's own internal inconsistency.
- ❌ ⚠️ **Calling Duncan Passage the Andaman-Nicobar divider.** ❗ The divider is the **Ten Degree Channel**, *"some 150 km wide"* per the Administration, **retrieved 04-10-2026**.
- ❌ ⚠️ **Confusing the Great Channel with the Ten Degree Channel**, or **Indira Point with Indira Col**. ❗ Both are standing map-bank traps.
- ❌ ⚠️ **Stating Saddle Peak's elevation without attribution.** ❗ **737 m is Khullar's**; the Survey of India value was not retrieved (U-05).
- ❌ ⚠️ **Writing "Port Blair" as the current capital.** ❗ **Sri Vijaya Puram** since **13 SEP 2024** (N-27).
- ❌ ⚠️ **Reproducing "Latitude 60 to 140 North"** from the official page. ❗ Read as **6° to 14° N**; the defect is disclosed, not reproduced (N-32).
- ❌ ⚠️ **Teaching island climate or monsoon rainfall.** 🔁 **2026 Prelims Q33 is `Geography/basic/16`'s.**
- ❌ ⚠️ **Writing "Wandoor Marine Biosphere Reserve".** ❗ The protected area at Wandoor is the **Mahatma Gandhi Marine National Park** (N-30).

### UPSC application

- **Syllabus:** ⚠️ Mapped clause, **first limb** — *geographical features and their location*. ❗ **The topic has no GS-III disaster mandate**, so seismic and tsunami material appears only as **exposure**.
- **Verified PYQ:** 🔁 ❗ **Three adjacent demands converge on this lesson and all three are routed**: **2018 Prelims Q85** (Barren Island) to `Geography/basic/03`; **2026 Prelims Q33** (A&N climate) to `Geography/basic/16`; **2025 GS-I Q7** (tsunamis) to `Disaster-Management/basic/06`, with its ledger inconsistency recorded as U-02. ❗ **None is claimed and no answer approach is offered for any of them.**
- **Traps:** ⚠️ The ten above. ❗ The coral-origin trap and the Barren Island trap are the two that an examiner can detect in a single sentence.
- **Answer use:** ❗ Open any Andaman and Nicobar question with the **structural sentence**, which carries four facts at once. ⚠️ *"The Andaman and Nicobar chain is a festoon continuing the Tertiary Arakan Yoma along the northern Sunda margin; its islands are built of tertiary sandstone, limestone and shale resting on basic and ultrabasic volcanics, which is why they carry real relief — Saddle Peak at 737 m in Khullar's account — and why their coral is a fringing veneer rather than their foundation."*

Revision notes:

- ✅ **The arc, from four convergent sources:** C2 §§1 and 3.1 (*"extension of the Tertiary **Arakan Yoma**"*); Majid 2.42 (*"extension of the Tertiary mountain chain of **Arakanyoma**"*); Khullar 89 (lithology); map bank (*"northern continuation"* of the **Sunda Trench**).
- ✅ **Lithology:** *"tertiary **sandstone, limestone and shale** resting on **basic and ultrabasic volcanoes**"* (Khullar, printed 89). ⚠️ **Fringing reefs sit ON this structure; they did not build it.**
- ❌ **[N-12]** Majid's *"the physiography of the Nicobar islands is **mainly of coral origin**"* is **a source defect**, contradicted by C2 line 21, Khullar's lithology and the map bank's *"Great Nicobar is not a coral atoll"*.
- ✅ **Relief:** **Saddle Peak, North Andaman, 737 m — KHULLAR'S figure**. ❗ **[U-05]** C2 line 25 says use Survey of India data; **it was not retrieved**; **no competing figure is stated**. ✅ **Dimensions (Khullar, printed 88-89):** archipelago **6° 45′ N-13° 45′ N**, **92° 10′ E-94° 15′ E**, about **590 km** long, max width about **58 km**; Andaman group **260 × 30 km**; Nicobar group **262 km**, max width **58 km**. ❗ **[N-33]** the **same 58 km** appears for both — flagged as a possible transcription artefact.
- ❌ **[N-32]** The Administration's *"Latitude **60 to 140** North"* is a **typographic defect** for **6° to 14° N**. ❗ Disclosed; not reproduced; not silently corrected.
- ❌ **[N-16]** **Barren Island = India's only *confirmed* active volcano; Narcondam = *not treated as currently active*.** ❗ C2 is internally inconsistent at lines 22, 23, 31, 47 and 56, and this is stated.
- ❌ **[N-17]** Khullar places the volcanic islands *"**north of Port Blair**"*; Majid *"within **80 km east** of the Andaman Islands"*. ⚠️ Map bank: Barren *"East of Middle Andaman"*, Narcondam *"North of Barren Island"* — **reconciles both**. ❗ **No distance asserted.**
- ⚠️ **Channels, with discriminators:** **Coco Channel** (Myanmar's Coco Is. ↔ North Andaman) · **Duncan Passage** (South Andaman ↔ Little Andaman, south of Rutland; ❗ **not the divider**) · **Ten Degree Channel** (❗ **the divider**, about 10° N, 📰 *"some 150 km wide"*, retrieved **04-10-2026**) · **Sombrero** and **Grand** Channels (internal Nicobar) · **Great Channel** (Great Nicobar ↔ Sumatra; ✅ Khullar: **147 km from Sumatra**).
- ❗ **Indira Point** = southernmost point of **Indian territory**, on Great Nicobar. ❗ **Not Indira Col.** ❗ **Kanyakumari is mainland India's southern tip.**
- ⚠️ **Exposure stated, hazard routed:** ✅ *"The entire region falls in a **major earthquake zone**"* (Majid 2.42; C2 §3.1); ✅ the **26 December 2004** Sumatra event in the **Sunda Trench** affected A&N and the Tamil Nadu coast (Majid IWG, PDF 142, ❗ **folio not read, U-15**); 📰 Great Nicobar is *"seismically sensitive and cyclone-prone"* (PIB, **1 May 2026**).
- 🔁 **Mechanism routes:** earthquake and volcano → `Geography/basic/03`; tsunami hazard and **2025 GS-I Q7** → `Disaster-Management/basic/06` (**U-02**); **island climate and 2026 Prelims Q33** → `Geography/basic/16`.
- 📰 **Settlement geography (ours):** population **3,79,944 (2011)**, literacy **86.27 per cent**, 2001 language shares **Bengali 26 · Hindi 18.23 · Tamil 17.68 · Telugu 12.81 · Malayalam 8.11 · Nicobarese 8.05**, **Andaman Creole Hindi** as a trade language (A&N Admin, retrieved **04-10-2026**); **3 districts, 9 tehsils** (NIC, 2021 path); ✅ Majid: tribal population *"fast dwindling"*, **Onges, Jarawas and Sentinelese** surviving; **242 bird species**; **Giant Robber Crab**.
- 🔁 **Tribal policy, rights and PVTG criteria → `Social-Justice/basic/08` and `Polity/basic/Scheduled-and-Tribal-Areas`.** 🔁 **Species status and designation → `Environment-and-Ecology/basic/07` and `basic/24`.** ❌ **[N-30]** *"Wandoor Marine Biosphere Reserve"* is **not reproduced**; the Wandoor protected area is the **Mahatma Gandhi Marine National Park**.
- ❌ **[N-27]** **Sri Vijaya Puram**, renamed from Port Blair, **PIB Release ID 2054647, MHA, 13 SEP 2024**. ❗ Used on every map here. 🔁 Colonial-legacy framing → History and Art and Culture. ❗ **[U-12]** Majid's printed **2.38-2.41 are physically absent**; only **2.42** is cited. ❗ **[U-13] No Khullar folio is computed anywhere.**
- 🔑 **Mnemonic for the channels, north to south — C-D-T-S-G-G:** **C**oco, **D**uncan, **T**en Degree, **S**ombrero, **G**rand, **G**reat.

### Concept check

**Question:** A candidate writes: "The Andaman and Nicobar Islands are coral islands in the Bay of Bengal, with Barren Island as India's only active volcano, separated from each other by the Duncan Passage; the capital is Port Blair." Identify every error, correct each, and state which corrections also require a routing.

**Model answer:** There are five errors and two of them require routings. First, **"coral islands" is wrong as a classification**. The chain is a festoon continuing the Tertiary Arakan Yoma, and four independent sources converge on that reading: the Advanced owner, Majid's *Geography of India*, Khullar's lithology — *"tertiary sandstone, limestone and shale resting on basic and ultrabasic volcanoes"* — and the map bank's note that the arc is the northern continuation of the Sunda Trench. Majid's separate sentence that the Nicobars are *"mainly of coral origin"* conflicts with all of these and is recorded as a source defect. Coral here is a **fringing veneer on a structure it did not build**. Second, **"India's only active volcano" is over-stated**. The defensible form is **India's only *confirmed* active volcano**, and the Advanced owner is itself internally inconsistent, warning at one line against a settled active-or-dormant binary and asserting one at two others. Third, **the Duncan Passage does not separate the two groups**. It lies within the Andaman group, between South Andaman and Little Andaman, south of Rutland. The Andaman-Nicobar divider is the **Ten Degree Channel**, which the Administration's page describes as *"some 150 km wide"*. Fourth, **the capital is Sri Vijaya Puram**, renamed from Port Blair by the decision recorded in PIB Release ID 2054647 of 13 SEP 2024; both books and the National Informatics Centre profile predate the change. Fifth, by implication, **the sentence treats the islands as a single undifferentiated group**, whereas the Andaman and Nicobar groups differ in count, area and even in the reliability of their reef mapping. Two corrections require routings: anything beyond Barren Island's location and island type — its eruption history and activity classification — belongs to `Geography/basic/03_Vulcanism-and-Earthquakes`, which also owns the 2018 Prelims demand on it; and the renaming's historical and commemorative argument belongs to Modern Indian History and Indian Art and Culture, Geography retaining only the current map label.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not infer an island's origin from the presence of coral on its shores. A fringing reef can grow on any suitable shallow substrate, including the flank of a folded sedimentary island on an active margin, and its presence says nothing about what the island is made of. The diagnostic is lithology and structure, not the biota attached to the shoreline.

**Original Mains practice [15 marks]:** Account for the physical character of the Andaman and Nicobar island system, and explain what its structural setting does and does not license a geographer to say. *(Account for — answer in about 250 words.)*

**Model (about 250 words):** The system is a festoon: a looped archipelago continuing a continental fold belt. The Advanced owner describes the Andamans as an extension of the Tertiary Arakan Yoma, Majid's *Geography of India* says they were *"formed by the extension of the Tertiary mountain chain of Arakanyoma"*, and the map bank records the arc as the northern continuation of the Sunda Trench. The lithology corroborates it: Khullar reports *"tertiary sandstone, limestone and shale resting on basic and ultrabasic volcanoes"*. Three consequences follow. The islands are **elongated** along the structural grain, running about 590 km in Khullar's account. They carry **real relief**, Saddle Peak reaching 737 m in North Andaman on his figure, which is impossible on a reef island. And their coral is a **fringing veneer**, not a foundation — so Majid's claim that the Nicobars are *"mainly of coral origin"* conflicts with the lithology, with the Advanced owner and with the map bank's own trap row, and is treated as a source defect. The structural setting also licenses a statement of **exposure**: the region, in Majid's words, *"falls in a major earthquake zone"*, and the 2004 Sumatra event in the Sunda Trench affected these islands directly. What it does **not** license is hazard teaching. Neither canonical owner is mapped to the disaster clause, so tsunami genesis, propagation, warning and institutional response are routed to Disaster Management, and volcanic mechanics on Barren Island to the vulcanism topic. Geography here states position, structure, rock, relief and exposure, and stops.

**Rubric (quantified, 15 marks):** **5** for the arc setting established from convergent sources rather than asserted, with the Arakan Yoma continuation and the Sunda-margin position both named; **4** for lithology and relief used as corroborating evidence, with the rock sequence quoted, Saddle Peak attributed to its book and the coral-origin claim identified as a source defect; **4** for the channel system with discriminators, in particular that the Ten Degree Channel is the divider and the Duncan Passage is not, and with the Great Channel and Indira Point correctly placed; **2** for exposure stated without hazard teaching, with at least one explicit routing by owner path. Deduct 3 for classifying the chain as coral; deduct 2 for explaining Barren Island's eruption history rather than routing it.

  .====================================================================.
  |  THE COUNTING-CONVENTION RULE, which is the lesson:                |
  |  AN ISLAND COUNT IS NOT A FACT ABOUT NATURE. IT IS A FACT ABOUT    |
  |  A DEFINITION.  Before any count is quoted it must state:          |
  |    (1) WHO counted      (2) WHEN                                    |
  |    (3) WHAT COUNTS as an island (islet? rock? submerged reef?)      |
  |    (4) WHETHER habitation is a filter                               |
  |    (5) WHAT THE RESIDUAL IS, if the parts do not sum to the total   |
  '===================================================================='

  THE TWO AREA LEDGERS — the same total, a different partition
  +-----------------+------------------+------------------+
  |                 | OFFICIAL         | KHULLAR, 88-89   |
  |                 | (A&N Admin)      | BOOK-ERA         |
  +-----------------+------------------+------------------+
  | ANDAMAN group   | 6,408 sq km      | 6,596 sq km      |
  | NICOBAR group   | 1,841 sq km      | 1,653 sq km      |
  | TOTAL           | 8,249 sq km      | 8,249 sq km      |
  +-----------------+------------------+------------------+
  ** THE TOTALS AGREE EXACTLY. THE PARTITION DIFFERS BY EXACTLY
     188 sq km, TRANSFERRED FROM NICOBAR TO ANDAMAN. **

  => an agreed total conceals a disagreed boundary. [N-08]
