```text
   BY THE ADJACENT STATES."
   C2 section 3.3: Khullar notes DYNAMIC DELTAIC islands near the
   Sundarbans, where channel migration, erosion and accretion can
   CREATE OR REMOVE low-lying bars and islands - the NEW MOORE /
   SOUTH TALPATTI case.
   ** THESE ARE DEPOSITIONAL ISLANDS. The three-way scheme of Lesson 1
      has NO CLASS FOR THEM, and that gap is why this inset exists. **
   ** NOTE: this inset is a NAME INVENTORY from one book page, NOT a
      located map. No coordinate, area or status is claimed for any
      feature on it. Netrani and Malvan are added from PIB 2080596
      (04 DEC 2024) as REEF SITES. **
```

⚠️ *The matrix is a comparison of definitions, not a map. The inset is a name inventory from a single book page plus two dated reef sites; **no position, area or status is claimed for any feature on it**.*

### 1. The intuition, before any terminology

⚠️ How many islands does India have in the Bay of Bengal? ⚠️ **265**, says one book. **836**, says the Administration. **38 are inhabited**, says the body text of its page — **31**, says the box on the same page.

❗ **None of these is a mistake.** ⚠️ They are answers to different questions: *how many islands big enough to name*, *how many land features of any size including rocks*, *how many are lived on*. ⚠️ The numbers diverge because **the definitions diverge**, and the definitions diverge because the counters wanted different things.

❗ **The rule this lesson exists to install is therefore simple and absolute: an island count is not a fact about nature; it is a fact about a definition.** ⚠️ Quote no count without saying **who counted, when, what counted as an island, and whether habitation was a filter**.

### 2. The Andaman and Nicobar counts, with the arithmetic checked

📰 **A&N Administration About page, retrieved 04-10-2026**, and 📰 the **NIC State Profile, 2021 upload path**, both give **836 islands, islets and rocky outcrops**, and an area of **8,249 sq km**.

✅ **Khullar, printed folio 88**, gives **265 big and small islands**, with **about 203** in the Andaman group and the Nicobar group comprising **7 big and 12 small**, plus *"several tiny islands"*.

❌ ❗ **The arithmetic does not close (N-06).** ⚠️ **203 + 7 + 12 = 222**, against a stated total of **265**. ❗ **Forty-three islands are unaccounted for by the stated parts**, and they sit inside the phrase *"several tiny islands"*. ❗ **Whenever Khullar's counts are shown, the residual is disclosed, and 265 is never presented as a sum of its stated parts.**

❌ ❗ **And the obsolescence (N-07).** ⚠️ **265 is a book-era figure.** ⚠️ The Administration gives **836 islands, islets and rocky outcrops**. ❗ **Khullar's figure may appear only as a dated, attributed contrast**, never as a current count — and the gap between 265 and 836 is **not** a discovery of 571 new islands but a **change of counting threshold**.

❌ ❗ **The official self-contradiction, reported and not resolved (N-26, U-16).** 📰 The **same** Administration page, at the **same retrieval**, carries **"Inhabited Islands 31"** in its profile box and *"some **38** are permanently inhabited"* in its body text. 📰 The **NIC State Profile** gives **38**. ❗ **This edition reports 38 as the body-text and NIC figure and 31 as the profile-box figure, both official, and chooses neither.** ⚠️ It is the **best available worked example** of why a count must name its source **and its location within that source**.

📰 ⚠️ A second official self-contradiction on the same page, carried for the same reason: the profile box reports a population of **4,34,192 (Est.), 2019** while the body reports **3,79,944 (2011 Census)**. ❗ **Different bases — an estimate and a census — and both are stated with their bases attached.**

⚠️ **The Nicobar count is the worked example the rule was built for (N-13).** ⚠️ **Three sources, three counts, one group:**

| ⚠️ Source | ⚠️ Count | ⚠️ Status |
|---|---|---|
| ✅ Khullar, printed 89 | **7 big and 12 small**, plus several tiny | ⚠️ Book-era, attributed |
| ✅ Majid, *Geography of India*, printed 2.42 | *"**18 islands**, only **11 inhabited**"* | ⚠️ Book-era, attributed |
| 📰 A&N Administration, retrieved 04-10-2026 | *"some **22 main islands (10 inhabited)**"* | ⚠️ **Official and governs** |

❗ **The official figure governs; both book figures are dated contrasts.** ⚠️ Note that even the three *inhabited* counts differ — **11** against **10** — and that the official word is *"main"* islands, which is itself a threshold statement.

### 3. The area ledger: agreement on the total, disagreement on the partition

| ⚠️ Quantity | 📰 Official (A&N Administration) | ✅ Khullar, printed 88-89 | ⚠️ Divergence |
|---|---|---|---|
| ⚠️ Andaman group | **6,408 sq km** | **6,596 sq km** | ⚠️ +188 in the book |
| ⚠️ Nicobar group | **1,841 sq km** | **1,653 sq km** | ⚠️ −188 in the book |
| ⚠️ **Total** | **8,249 sq km** | **8,249 sq km** | ⚠️ **Zero** |

❌ ❗ **This is the most elegant single finding in the India half of the edition (N-08).** ⚠️ **Both splits sum to exactly 8,249 sq km, and the internal partition differs by exactly 188 sq km, transferred from Nicobar to Andaman.** ❗ **The totals agree; the boundary does not.**

⚠️ **What that implies, stated carefully.** ⚠️ The two sources are measuring the **same archipelago** — hence the identical total — but **assigning at least one island, or a group of islands, to different groups**. ⚠️ A candidate who notices this has demonstrated that **an aggregate can be stable while its components are contested**, which is the same lesson Lesson 10 taught about reef area and Lesson 16 will teach about project land.

❗ **Teach the agreement on the total and the disagreement on the partition. Do not average. Do not choose.**

### 4. The Lakshadweep counts, and the factor of 3.40

| ⚠️ Quantity | 📰 Administration of Lakshadweep, retrieved this audit | ✅ Khullar, printed 89 | ✅ Majid, *Geography of India*, 2.42 |
|---|---|---|---|
| ⚠️ Land area | **32 sq km** | **108.78 sq km** | — |
| ⚠️ Islands | **12 atolls, 3 reefs, 5 submerged banks**; composed of **10 inhabited islands + 17 uninhabited islands and attached islets + 4 newly formed islets + 5 submerged reefs** | **25 small islands** | **43 islands in the Arabian Sea, 10 inhabited** |
| ⚠️ Largest island | — | **Minicoy, 4.53 sq km** | **Minicoy, 4.5 sq km** |
| ⚠️ Smallest inhabited | **Bitra**, population **271 (Census 2011)** | *"**Betra**"*, **0.12 sq km** | — |
| ⚠️ Distance to mainland | **220 to 440 km from Kochi** | **200-500 km** south-west of the Kerala coast | *"about **109 km from Calicut**"* |

❌ ❗ **The area divergence (N-09).** ⚠️ **Khullar's 108.78 sq km is 3.40 times the official land area of 32 sq km.** ⚠️ The most probable reconciliation — and this edition states it as an inference, not as a finding — is that the two are **measuring different things**: a land area against something larger, perhaps including reef flat or lagoon enclosure. ❗ **But no source retrieved in this audit says so**, so the divergence is reported with both figures attributed and **the ratio stated**, and no reconciliation is asserted.

❌ ❗ **The distance divergence (N-10).** ⚠️ Majid's *"about 109 km from Calicut"* is irreconcilable with the official **220 to 440 km from Kochi**, and Khullar's **200-500 km** brackets the official range loosely. ❗ **Both book figures appear only as dated, attributed contrasts.** ⚠️ Note that Majid's *"10 inhabited"* **does** agree with the official figure, which is worth saying: the books are not uniformly wrong, and treating them as uniformly wrong is its own error.

❗ ⚠️ **The 36 that is not in any single sentence.** ⚠️ The conventional figure of **36** for Lakshadweep is the **sum of the Administration's own composition**: 10 + 17 + 4 + 5 = **36**. ❗ **It is a derived total, and this edition derives it explicitly rather than quoting it bare**, because that is exactly what the counting-convention rule demands.

### 5. Spelling, naming and the discipline that goes with them

❌ ❗ **Bitra, not "Betra" (N-11).** ⚠️ **Khullar's text prints *"Betra"***; 📰 the **Administration prints Bitra**, its smallest inhabited island, population **271 in Census 2011**. ❗ **Use the official spelling; record the book's as a variant.**

❌ ❗ **Amindivi, not "Amendivi" (N-11).** ⚠️ **Khullar's text prints *"Amendivi Islands"***, while ✅ **his own Fig. 3.15 prints *AMINDIVI ISLANDS***. ❗ **The book disagrees with itself on the same spread**, and the official form is **Amindivi**.

❌ ❗ **Sri Vijaya Puram, not Port Blair (N-27).** 📰 **PIB Release ID 2054647, MHA, 13 SEP 2024.** ⚠️ **Both books and the NIC State Profile print the old name**; the Administration's current page uses the new one. ❗ **The NIC profile is not an error — it is a version trap**, and the distinction between an error and a version is itself examinable.

❌ ❗ **Mahatma Gandhi Marine National Park, not "Wandoor Marine Biosphere Reserve" (N-30).** ⚠️ **Majid, printed 2.42, uses the latter name**; the map bank records the protected area at Wandoor in South Andaman as the **Mahatma Gandhi Marine National Park**, and the biosphere reserve on Great Nicobar is a **separate designation**. ❗ **The book's name is not reproduced.** 🔁 Designation detail routes to `Environment-and-Ecology/basic/07_Biosphere-Reserves-and-Ramsar-Sites`.

❌ ❗ **No area may be computed from dimensions (N-29).** ⚠️ **Khullar gives Great Nicobar as "50 × 25 km".** ⚠️ **A rectangle is not an island.** ⚠️ The product, 1,250 sq km, would sit against Khullar's own **Nicobar group total of 1,653 sq km**, leaving only **403 sq km** for every other Nicobar island — which is implausible on its face. ❗ **The only verified statement about Great Nicobar's area is ICRZ 2019's: a geographical area of *"more than 1,000 sq.km"* (U-06).** ❗ **No area is computed from the dimensions anywhere in this edition.**

### 6. 🇮🇳 India hook — the depositional limb the classification omits

🇮🇳 ⚠️ Lesson 1 recorded that Leong's three-way scheme has **no class for depositional islands**. ❗ **This is where the gap is filled**, because any Indian island count that stops at the two archipelagos is understating the inventory on a definitional ground it has not declared.

✅ **Majid, *Geography of India*, printed folio 2.42, "Offshore Islands"**: *"There are numerous islands in the **delta region of Ganga** and in the **Gulf of Mannar**. Among the Western coast islands **Piram, Bhaisala (Kathiawar), Diu, Vaida, Nora, Pirtan, Karunbhar (Kachchh coast), Khadiabet, Aliabet (Narmada-Tapi mouths), Butchers, Elephanta, Karanja, Cross (near Mumbai), Bhatkal, Pegioncock, St. Mary (Mangalore coast), Anjidiv (Goa coast), Vypin near Kochi, Pamban, Crocodile, Adunda (Gulf of Mannar), Sri Harikota (mouth of Pulicat Lake), Pairkud (mouth of Chilka Lake), Short, Wheeler (Mahanadi-Brahmani mouth), and New Moore, and Ganga-Sagar and Sagar (Ganga Delta). Many of these islands are uninhabited and administered by the adjacent states.**"*

✅ **C2 §3.3** adds the dynamic limb: Khullar notes *"dynamic deltaic islands near the Sundarbans, where channel migration, erosion and accretion can create or remove low-lying bars/islands (the **New Moore / South Talpatti** example)"*.

⚠️ **Three analytical points, which are what make this more than a list.**

1. ⚠️ **These islands are genetically distinct.** ⚠️ They are **depositional** — built by rivers, tides and longshore drift — and they belong to neither of Leong's two primary classes. ❗ A complete Indian island classification therefore has **four** limbs: continental-festoon, volcanic, coral and **depositional**.
2. ⚠️ **They are impermanent by construction.** ⚠️ Channel migration, erosion and accretion can **create or remove** them, which is exactly what the New Moore / South Talpatti case demonstrates. ❗ **An island count in a delta is a count at a date**, and this is the sharpest possible illustration of the counting-convention rule.
3. ⚠️ **They are administratively different.** ✅ Majid: *"administered by the adjacent states"* — unlike the two Union Territories, which is a fact of political geography that follows from their genetic class.

⚠️ 📰 Two further features belong on this inset from a dated official source: **Malvan, Maharashtra** and **Netrani**, both recorded as reef sites in **PIB Release ID 2080596, MoES, 04 DEC 2024**, and **Majuli**, the Brahmaputra river island, which is a fluvial depositional island of the same genetic family.

🔁 ❗ **The route-out this lesson must perform.** ⚠️ **Island endemism as biogeography, hotspot designation and species status are not Topic 11's.** 🔁 They route to **`Geography/basic/37_Cultural-and-Social-Geography-of-India` §12.6**, which carries the isolation-to-endemism chain, and to **`Environment-and-Ecology/basic/05_IUCN-Red-List-and-Endemism`**. ❗ The repository's answer-worthiness audit records an unresolved failure on biodiversity hotspots and island biogeography with the remedy placed in `basic/37`; ❗ **Topic 11 does not inherit that demand and does not attempt to cure it.**

### 7. Comparison, objection and the residual

| ⚠️ Divergence type | ⚠️ Example in this lesson | ⚠️ Correct handling |
|---|---|---|
| ⚠️ **Threshold divergence** | 836 against 265 | ⚠️ Name the threshold: islets and rocky outcrops counted or not |
| ⚠️ **Filter divergence** | 836 against 38 | ⚠️ Name the filter: habitation |
| ⚠️ **Internal inconsistency in one source** | 38 against 31 on one page | ❗ Report both with their locations; **choose neither** |
| ⚠️ **Non-closing parts** | 203 + 7 + 12 = 222 against 265 | ❗ Disclose the **43-island residual** |
| ⚠️ **Partition divergence under an agreed total** | 6,596/1,653 against 6,408/1,841, both summing to 8,249 | ❗ State the **exact 188 sq km transfer** |
| ⚠️ **Scale-of-measurement divergence** | 108.78 against 32 sq km | ❗ State the **3.40 ratio**; assert no reconciliation |
| ⚠️ **Version divergence** | Port Blair against Sri Vijaya Puram | ❗ Use the current name; record the change with its date |
| ⚠️ **Orthographic divergence** | *"Betra"* / Bitra; *"Amendivi"* / Amindivi | ❗ Official spelling; book form as a variant |

**Strongest objection.** ⚠️ This is bookkeeping, not geography. An examiner wants to know about islands, not about how different agencies count them.

**Reply.** ⚠️ The objection fails on its own terms in this topic, because **the counts differ by factors, not margins** — 836 against 265, 108.78 against 32 — and a candidate who quotes one without knowing the others will state something an examiner can falsify from a government website. ❗ More importantly, **the counting-convention rule is load-bearing for two later lessons**. ⚠️ Lesson 14 must handle Lakshadweep's 25-versus-36-versus-43 problem. ⚠️ **Lesson 16's central official statistic — that the Great Nicobar project occupies "approximately 2 per cent of the total area of Andaman and Nicobar Islands" — is itself a counting-convention claim**, since it divides by a total that this lesson has just shown to be partitioned differently by different sources. ❗ The skill is not bookkeeping; it is **knowing what a quoted ratio is a ratio of**.

❓ **Residual, which this edition does not close.** ⚠️ **No reconciliation is available for the 3.40 ratio between Khullar's Lakshadweep area and the official one**, and none is asserted. ⚠️ ❓ **The 188 sq km partition divergence is stated but not attributed to any named island or group**, because no source identifies which feature moved. ⚠️ ❓ **The 43-island residual in Khullar's Andaman and Nicobar count is disclosed but not resolved**, since the book gives no breakdown of *"several tiny islands"*.

### 8. Traps specific to this lesson

- ❌ ⚠️ **Quoting any island count bare.** ❗ Who, when, what counts, what filter, what residual.
- ❌ ⚠️ **Presenting 265 as a sum of its parts.** ❗ 203 + 7 + 12 = **222**; **43 are unstated** (N-06).
- ❌ ⚠️ **Presenting 265 as a current figure.** ❗ Book-era; the official count is **836 islands, islets and rocky outcrops** (N-07).
- ❌ ⚠️ **Choosing between 38 and 31 inhabited islands.** ❗ **Both are on the same official page**; report both (N-26, U-16).
- ❌ ⚠️ **Averaging the two area splits.** ❗ The totals agree exactly; the partition differs by exactly **188 sq km** (N-08).
- ❌ ⚠️ **Using 108.78 sq km for Lakshadweep.** ❗ Official land area **32 sq km**; the book figure is **3.40 times** larger (N-09).
- ❌ ⚠️ **Writing "Betra", "Amendivi", "Port Blair" or "Wandoor Marine Biosphere Reserve".** ❗ Bitra, Amindivi, **Sri Vijaya Puram**, **Mahatma Gandhi Marine National Park** (N-11, N-27, N-30).
- ❌ ⚠️ **Computing Great Nicobar's area from "50 × 25 km".** ❗ Only ICRZ 2019's *">1000 sq.km"* may be stated (N-29, U-06).
- ❌ ⚠️ **Stopping the island inventory at the two archipelagos.** ❗ The **depositional limb** exists and the classification omits it.
- ❌ ⚠️ **Arguing island endemism as biogeography.** 🔁 `Geography/basic/37` §12.6 and `Environment-and-Ecology/basic/05`.

### UPSC application

- **Syllabus:** ⚠️ Mapped clause, **first and second limbs** — *geographical features and their location* and *changes in critical geographical features*. ⚠️ Depositional islands that can be *"created or removed"* are the clause's second limb in its most literal form.
- **Verified PYQ:** ⚠️ **No owned demand is a counting demand.** ❗ **O1's key is unavailable locally and none is inferred.** ⚠️ The lesson's examination value is **protective**: it prevents the quotation of an obsolete count as a current fact, which is the most easily detected error a candidate can make in an Indian island answer.
- **Traps:** ⚠️ The ten above. ❗ The bare-count trap and the Lakshadweep area trap are the two most likely to appear.
- **Answer use:** ❗ When a count is needed, **give it with its convention in the same sentence**. ⚠️ *"The Andaman and Nicobar Administration records 836 islands, islets and rocky outcrops over 8,249 sq km, of which its body text says some 38 are permanently inhabited while its own profile box says 31; older textbook figures of 265 islands count only named islands and their stated parts sum to 222, leaving 43 undisclosed."*

Revision notes:

- ❗ **The rule:** an island count is **a fact about a definition**, not about nature. ❗ State **who · when · what counts · what filter · what residual**.
- 📰 **A&N official:** **836 islands, islets and rocky outcrops**; **8,249 sq km**; Andaman **6,408**, Nicobar **1,841**; Nicobars *"some **22 main islands (10 inhabited)**"*; Ten Degree Channel *"some 150 km wide"*. Retrieved **04-10-2026**.
- ✅ **Khullar, printed 88-89:** **265** islands; about **203** Andaman; Nicobar **7 big + 12 small** plus *"several tiny"*; Andaman **6,596**, Nicobar **1,653**.
- ❌ **[N-06]** **203 + 7 + 12 = 222 against 265 → 43 unaccounted.** ❗ Never present 265 as a sum of its parts. ❌ **[N-07]** 265 is **book-era**; the official count is **836 islands, islets and rocky outcrops** — a **threshold** difference, not a discovery.
- ❌ **[N-08]** **Both area splits total 8,249 exactly; the partition differs by exactly 188 sq km**, transferred from Nicobar to Andaman. ❗ **Agreement on the total, disagreement on the boundary.**
- ❌ **[N-26 / U-16]** **38** inhabited in the Administration's body text and in the NIC profile; **31** in the Administration's **own profile box**. ❗ **Both reported; neither chosen.** ⚠️ Same page also gives population **4,34,192 (Est.) 2019** against **3,79,944 (2011 Census)** — **different bases, both stated**.
- ❌ **[N-13]** **Nicobar count, three ways:** Khullar **7 + 12**; Majid **18, 11 inhabited**; official *"some **22 main** (10 inhabited)"*. ❗ **The official figure governs; the books are dated contrasts.**
- 📰 **Lakshadweep official:** **12 atolls, 3 reefs, 5 submerged banks**; **10 inhabited + 17 uninhabited islands and attached islets + 4 newly formed islets + 5 submerged reefs = 36**; land **32 sq km**; **Bitra** smallest inhabited, population **271 (Census 2011)**; **220-440 km from Kochi**.
- ❌ **[N-09]** **Khullar: 25 small islands over 108.78 sq km** — ❗ **3.40 times** the official 32 sq km; no reconciliation asserted. ❌ **[N-10]** **Majid: 43 islands, 10 inhabited; *"about 109 km from Calicut"*** against official **220-440 km from Kochi**. ⚠️ **The "10 inhabited" does agree** — the books are not uniformly wrong.
- ❌ **[N-11]** **Bitra**, not *"Betra"*; **Amindivi**, not *"Amendivi"* — ❗ and **Khullar's own Fig. 3.15 prints AMINDIVI**, contradicting his text. ❌ **[N-30]** **Mahatma Gandhi Marine National Park**, not *"Wandoor Marine Biosphere Reserve"*.
- ❌ **[N-29 / U-06]** ❗ **No area is computed from Khullar's "50 × 25 km" for Great Nicobar.** ⚠️ 1,250 against his own Nicobar total of 1,653 would leave **403 sq km** for all other Nicobar islands. ❗ Only ICRZ 2019's *">1000 sq.km"* is stated.
- ⚠️ **The depositional limb (Majid 2.42, "Offshore Islands"):** Ganga delta and Gulf of Mannar islands; **Piram, Bhaisala, Diu, Vaida, Nora, Pirtan, Karunbhar, Khadiabet, Aliabet, Butchers, Elephanta, Karanja, Cross, Bhatkal, Pegioncock, St. Mary, Anjidiv, Vypin, Pamban, Crocodile, Adunda, Sri Harikota, Pairkud, Short, Wheeler, New Moore, Ganga-Sagar and Sagar**; *"many ... uninhabited and **administered by the adjacent states**"*. ⚠️ Plus **Majuli** (fluvial) and, from PIB 2080596 (**04 DEC 2024**), the reef sites **Malvan** and **Netrani**.
- ✅ **C2 §3.3:** dynamic deltaic islands near the Sundarbans — **New Moore / South Talpatti** — can be **created or removed**. ❗ **A delta island count is a count at a date.**
- ❗ **A complete Indian classification has FOUR limbs:** continental-festoon · volcanic · coral · **depositional**. 🔁 **Island endemism as biogeography → `Geography/basic/37` §12.6 and `Environment-and-Ecology/basic/05_IUCN-Red-List-and-Endemism`.** ❗ Topic 11 does not cure the audit's open biogeography failure.
- 🔑 **Mnemonic — "W-W-W-F-R": Who · When · What counts · Filter · Residual.**

### Concept check

**Question:** A candidate writes: "India's Andaman and Nicobar Islands number 265, of which 36 are inhabited, covering 8,249 sq km with 6,596 sq km in the Andaman group; the capital is Port Blair and Lakshadweep consists of 25 islands over 108.78 sq km." Audit this sentence completely.

**Model answer:** Seven defects, of which four are obsolescence, two are conflation and one is arithmetic. **First**, 265 is a book-era count of *"big and small islands"*; the Andaman and Nicobar Administration records **836 islands, islets and rocky outcrops**, so the two differ by a counting threshold and not by a discovery. **Second**, the stated total does not close: 203 Andaman plus 7 big and 12 small Nicobar is 222, so **43 islands sit inside the phrase "several tiny islands"** and the figure cannot be presented as a sum of its parts. **Third**, 36 is not the Andaman and Nicobar inhabited count at all — it is the conventional Lakshadweep composition total, derived as 10 inhabited plus 17 uninhabited islands and attached islets plus 4 newly formed islets plus 5 submerged reefs. The Administration's own page gives **38** permanently inhabited in its body text and **31** in its profile box, and both must be reported because both are official and they contradict each other. **Fourth**, the total area of 8,249 sq km is correct and agrees with the official figure exactly, but the partition does not: the book's 6,596 and 1,653 against the official 6,408 and 1,841 differ by **exactly 188 sq km**, transferred from Nicobar to Andaman — an agreed total concealing a disagreed boundary. **Fifth**, the capital is **Sri Vijaya Puram**, renamed per PIB Release ID 2054647 of 13 SEP 2024. **Sixth**, Lakshadweep's land area is officially **32 sq km**; the book's 108.78 sq km is **3.40 times** larger, and no source reconciles them. **Seventh**, "25 islands" is one of three incompatible counts — 25 in Khullar, 43 in Majid's *Geography of India*, and an official composition yielding 36 — so it may appear only as a dated, attributed contrast. The corrected sentence would name each count's authority, date and basis rather than presenting any of them bare.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not treat a divergence between a book and an official source as evidence that the book is simply wrong. Khullar's *"10 inhabited"* for Lakshadweep agrees with the Administration exactly, and his Andaman and Nicobar total of 8,249 sq km is identical to the official one. The books are obsolete on thresholds, names and some areas, and correct elsewhere, so each figure must be checked individually rather than the source being discarded wholesale.

**Original Mains practice [15 marks]:** "Every Indian island count in circulation counts something different." Examine this statement and explain why the point matters beyond pedantry. *(Examine — answer in about 250 words.)*

**Model (about 250 words):** The statement is demonstrable. The Andaman and Nicobar Administration records **836 islands, islets and rocky outcrops**, a maximal count admitting rock outcrops; Khullar records **265 big and small islands**, excluding features below an unstated threshold, and his own parts sum to 222, leaving 43 undisclosed inside *"several tiny islands"*. Habitation filters produce further figures: the Administration's body text gives **38** permanently inhabited and its own profile box **31**, an official self-contradiction on a single page. Lakshadweep is worse. Its Administration describes **12 atolls, three reefs and five submerged banks**, composed of 10 inhabited islands, 17 uninhabited islands and attached islets, 4 newly formed islets and 5 submerged reefs — a composition summing to 36 — while Khullar gives **25 small islands** over 108.78 sq km against an official land area of **32 sq km**, a factor of 3.40, and Majid's *Geography of India* gives **43 islands in the Arabian Sea**. Areas diverge in a subtler way: the official Andaman-Nicobar split of 6,408 and 1,841 and Khullar's 6,596 and 1,653 both total **8,249 sq km exactly**, differing by **exactly 188 sq km** in partition alone. The point matters because quoted ratios inherit these conventions. The Great Nicobar project is officially described as occupying *"approximately 2 per cent of the total area of Andaman and Nicobar Islands"*, a claim that depends entirely on which total is used — and the related forest-diversion figure of 1.82 per cent uses the forest area of the whole Union Territory, not of the island.

**Rubric (quantified, 15 marks):** **6** for the counting-convention rule stated as a general principle and applied, with the five components — authority, date, threshold, filter and residual — demanded of any quoted count; **4** for the obsolescence ledger, with at least three book-versus-official divergences given with both figures attributed and the book never presented as current; **3** for the area-split divergence handled exactly, with the agreed total and the precise 188 sq km partition transfer identified; **2** for spelling and naming discipline, covering Bitra, Amindivi and Sri Vijaya Puram, and for refusing to compute an area from stated dimensions. Deduct 3 for choosing between the two official inhabited-island counts; deduct 2 for using 108.78 sq km as Lakshadweep's area.

## Lesson 14 — The Arabian Sea island system: atoll, lagoon, territorial water, exclusive economic zone

⚠️ *M4 is schematic. Island positions, spacing and shapes are indicative and not to projection; the cross-section is generic and follows Khullar's attributed description. Reef regions are **M5**, Lesson 11; the Bay of Bengal system is **M3**, Lesson 12.*

### 1. The intuition, before any terminology

⚠️ Lesson 12 described a mountain range with its feet in the sea. ❗ **This lesson describes its opposite in every respect**, in the same country, under the same flag, about a thousand kilometres away.

⚠️ There are **no hills**. ⚠️ There are **no streams and no valleys**. ⚠️ Nothing rises more than about five metres. ⚠️ The land is not rock in the ordinary sense — it is **carbonate the reef made**. ⚠️ And the land is the **smallest** part of the system by a very long way: the lagoon is over a hundred times the land, and the maritime jurisdiction is over twelve thousand times.

❗ **That last fact is the lesson's title in disguise.** ⚠️ **Lakshadweep is India's smallest Union Territory by land and one of its largest by sea**, and the reason is purely geometrical: a scatter of small islands spread over a wide area generates a very large maritime space from a very small land base.

### 2. Atoll geography, with the scale discipline applied

✅ **C2 §2 and §3.2**: Lakshadweep is *"entirely coral origin; atolls"*, and *"its atolls illustrate the coral-reef sequence linked with Darwin's subsidence theory in the basic chapter"*.

✅ **Khullar, printed folio 89**: all the islands *"are tiny islands of **coral origin and are surrounded by fringing reefs**"*.

❌ ❗ **The scale correction, restated here because this is where it bites (N-34).** ⚠️ **Both statements are true at different scales.**

| ⚠️ Scale | ⚠️ What the correct term is | ⚠️ Why |
|---|---|---|
| ⚠️ **Archipelago** | ⚠️ **Atoll archipelago** | 📰 The Administration describes **12 atolls, three reefs and five submerged banks** |
| ⚠️ **Reef tract** | ⚠️ **Atoll** | ⚠️ A ring of reef enclosing a lagoon, with islands on the rim — Lesson 6's discriminator |
| ⚠️ **Individual island** | ⚠️ **Fringing reef on the seaward face** | ✅ Khullar's description, and consistent with Lesson 6: an island on an atoll rim has its own shore and its own shore-attached reef |

❗ **C1's scale control at lines 217-220 makes it mandatory to state the scale with the term**, and this edition does so every time. ❗ **"Lakshadweep is an atoll archipelago whose individual islands carry fringing reef on their seaward faces" is the full, correct and non-contradictory formulation.**

⚠️ ❗ **And the limit on the Darwin connection, carried forward from Lesson 7.** ⚠️ C2 links Lakshadweep's atolls to Darwin's sequence. ⚠️ **The form matches the sequence's terminal state** — ring, lagoon, no central land. ❗ **But no primary evidence of Lakshadweep's foundation was retrieved in this audit, so this edition claims a morphological fit and says nothing whatever about what lies beneath.**

### 3. The internal division at 11° N

✅ **Khullar, printed folio 89**: *"The islands north of **11 N** are known as **Amendivi Islands** while those south of this latitude are called **Cannanore Islands**. In the extreme south is the **Minicoy** island."*

⚠️ **Three groups, one latitude line and one outlier** — which is a genuinely useful organising device for an archipelago whose individual names are otherwise hard to hold.

| ⚠️ Division | ⚠️ Position | ⚠️ Members named on M4 |
|---|---|---|
| ⚠️ **Amindivi group** | ⚠️ **North of 11° N** | ⚠️ Chetlat, **Bitra**, Kiltan, Kadmat, Amini |
| ⚠️ **Cannanore group** | ⚠️ **South of 11° N** | ⚠️ Agatti, Bangaram, **Kavaratti**, Androth, Kalpeni |
| ⚠️ **Minicoy** | ⚠️ **Extreme south**, beyond the Nine Degree Channel | ⚠️ Minicoy alone |

❌ ❗ **The spelling, corrected (N-11).** ⚠️ **Khullar's text prints "Amendivi"; his own Fig. 3.15 prints "AMINDIVI ISLANDS"**, and the official form is **Amindivi**. ❗ **The book contradicts itself on the same spread**, and this edition uses the official spelling and records the variant.

⚠️ **Two channels close the system southward**, and both are standing traps:

- ⚠️ **Nine Degree Channel** — between **Minicoy** and the **main Lakshadweep group**. ❗ **It does not separate Andaman from Nicobar**; that is the Ten Degree Channel of Lesson 12.
- ✅ **Eight Degree Channel** — between **Minicoy** and the **Maldives**, named by Majid at printed 2.42. ❗ **It is not within the main Lakshadweep cluster.**

### 4. The nested-area hierarchy, which is the lesson's central payload

📰 **Administration of Lakshadweep, "Islands" page, retrieved this audit**:

| 📰 Space | 📰 Area | ⚠️ Multiple of land area |
|---|---|---|
| 📰 **Land** | **32 sq km** | ⚠️ 1 |
| 📰 **Lagoon** | about **4,200 sq km** | ⚠️ about **131 ×** |
| 📰 **Territorial waters** | **20,000 sq km** | ⚠️ **625 ×** |
| 📰 **Economic zone** | about **4 lakh sq km** | ⚠️ about **12,500 ×** |

❗ **Four nested spaces, each an order of magnitude or more larger than the last.** ⚠️ **This is the single most arresting quantitative fact in the India half of the topic**, and it is entirely official and retrievable.

⚠️ **The geographical explanation, which is what converts the table into an argument:**

1. ⚠️ **The lagoon is large because the islands sit on atoll rims.** ⚠️ An atoll's enclosed water is part of the reef structure, so a small amount of land implies a large amount of enclosed shallow water **by construction**.
2. ⚠️ **The territorial and economic spaces are large because the islands are dispersed.** ⚠️ Maritime zones are generated from baselines around each island, and a scatter of islands across a wide area generates far more sea than a single compact island of the same total land area. ❗ **Dispersion, not size, is what produces maritime extent.**
3. ⚠️ **The consequence is a jurisdictional inversion**: the Union Territory with the least land has one of the largest maritime responsibilities, with the smallest population base to administer it — 📰 **64,429 in Census 2011**, per the same page.

🔁 ❗ **And the boundary, stated at the point of temptation.** ⚠️ **The figures above are the Administration's stated areas and are used as such.** ❗ **UNCLOS, the territorial sea, the contiguous zone and the exclusive economic zone *as law*, baselines, maritime delimitation and any strategic argument built on them are not Topic 11's.** 🔁 They route to **`International-Relations/basic/04_Indo-Pacific-Indian-Ocean-and-Maritime-Security`**, which also owns the 2022 Prelims demands on UNCLOS and on the Senkaku dispute and the 2024 demand on the Maldives.

### 5. The asymmetry, and what it tells you about process

✅ **Khullar, printed folio 89**: *"Shallow lagoons are seen on their **western side**, while on the **eastern seaboard the slopes are steeper**."*

⚠️ **Read against Lesson 9's budget section, this is a statement about where sediment goes.** ⚠️ A reef island on an atoll rim sits between an **ocean-facing side** and a **lagoon-facing side**. ⚠️ The ocean-facing side receives the open-sea wave energy; the lagoon-facing side is sheltered. ⚠️ Sediment produced on the reef is driven **across the reef flat** and accumulates on the **sheltered** side, producing a gentle slope into shallow water, while the exposed side remains steeper and more erosional.

❗ **The detail is India-specific, book-sourced, process-explicable and almost never written in answers**, which makes it high-value. ⚠️ ❓ **This edition does not assert a cause for the specific west-east orientation Khullar records**, because the source gives none and no corroborating primary was retrieved; it states the observed asymmetry and the general process that produces such asymmetries.

✅ **The relief, from the same page**: the islands *"do not rise more than five metre above sea level"*, their topography is *"flat"*, and *"relief features such as hills, streams, valleys, etc. are **conspicuous by their absence**"*. ❗ **No streams means no surface freshwater**, which is why Lesson 9's lens argument is not an abstraction here but the whole water supply.

### 6. 🇮🇳 India hook — the whole lesson, with its settlement geography and its boundary

🇮🇳 📰 **The ten inhabited islands and the official composition**, from the Administration of Lakshadweep's page, retrieved this audit: **12 atolls, three reefs and five submerged banks**, comprising **10 inhabited islands, 17 uninhabited islands and attached islets, 4 newly formed islets and 5 submerged reefs** — the composition that yields the conventional **36**, derived at Lesson 13 rather than quoted bare.

🇮🇳 📰 **Settlement and locational geography, which is ours:**

- 📰 **Population 64,429 (Census 2011)**; **Kavaratti** is the capital (✅ also in Majid, printed 2.42).
- 📰 **Bitra** is the smallest inhabited island, population **271 (Census 2011)**.
- 📰 **Bangaram**, an **uninhabited** island, was **enumerated in 2011 with a population of 61** — ⚠️ a precise illustration of why "inhabited" is a **definitional** category and not a physical one, exactly as Lesson 13 argued.
- 📰 **Coordinates 8° to 12° 13′ N, 71° to 74° E**; ❌ ❗ **the page prints "12º 13″" with a seconds mark where minutes is meant (N-32)**. ❗ **This edition reads it as 12° 13′ N, discloses the typographic defect, and neither reproduces the defective form nor silently corrects it.**
- ❌ ❗ **The same page calls the same five features "submerged banks" in one sentence and "submerged reefs" in the next.** ❗ Disclosed; not reproduced as if both were separate categories.
- 📰 **Languages:** Malayalam throughout *"**except Minicoy where people speak Mahl which is written in Divehi script** and is spoken in **Maldives** also"*.
- 📰 ✅ **Occupations:** fishing, coconut cultivation and coir twisting (Administration); ✅ Majid, printed 2.42: *"**coconut is the only major crop**"* and *"**fishing is the main occupation**"*; **Minicoy** *"has a **light house and a weather observatory**"*.

❗ ⚠️ **The Minicoy linguistic fact is genuine island geography and is worth a sentence in an answer.** ⚠️ Minicoy lies **south of the Nine Degree Channel** and **north of the Eight Degree Channel**, closer in sea distance to the Maldives than to the main Lakshadweep cluster. ⚠️ Its language is **Mahl**, written in **Divehi script**, and is spoken in the Maldives. ❗ **Linguistic affinity has followed sea distance rather than administrative boundary**, which is Lesson 2's isolation argument working in reverse — **connection by sea where the sea is the easier route**.

🔁 ❗ **The route-out this lesson must perform.** 📰 The Administration records that *"The **entire indigenous population has been classified as Scheduled Tribes**"* under the Scheduled Castes and Scheduled Tribes (modification orders) of **1956**, and that *"There are **no Scheduled Castes** in this Union Territory"*. ❗ **Those are stated once as dated official facts of administrative geography.** ❗ **Tribal classification policy, criteria, welfare provision and constitutional treatment are not Topic 11's** and route to **`Social-Justice/basic/08_Scheduled-Tribes-PVTGs-and-Tribal-Welfare`** and **`Polity/basic/Scheduled-and-Tribal-Areas`**, which own the live 2026 GS-II tribal-development demand.

### 7. Comparison, objection and the residual

| | ⚠️ Lakshadweep (this lesson) | ⚠️ Andaman and Nicobar (Lesson 12) |
|---|---|---|
| ⚠️ Genetic class | ⚠️ **Oceanic, coral** | ⚠️ **Continental, festoon** |
| ⚠️ Reef relation | ⚠️ The islands **are** the reef's product | ⚠️ Reefs are a **veneer** on a structure they did not build |
| ⚠️ Relief | ✅ *"Not more than five metre"*; no hills, streams or valleys | ✅ **Saddle Peak 737 m** (Khullar) |
| ⚠️ Land area | 📰 **32 sq km** | 📰 **8,249 sq km** |
| ⚠️ Internal division | ✅ **11° N**: Amindivi north, Cannanore south; Minicoy beyond the Nine Degree Channel | ⚠️ **Ten Degree Channel**: Andaman north, Nicobar south |
| ⚠️ Freshwater | ⚠️ **Lens only** — no surface water at all | ⚠️ Surface drainage exists on larger islands |
| ⚠️ Principal exposure | ⚠️ **Reef health and lens integrity** — Lesson 9; **84.6 per cent bleaching 2023-24** | ⚠️ **Seismic**, on an active margin |
| ⚠️ Maritime inversion | ⚠️ Land **32 sq km**, economic zone about **4 lakh sq km** — about **12,500 ×** | ⚠️ Not computed here; no official nested series was retrieved for A&N |

**Strongest objection.** ⚠️ An archipelago of 32 square kilometres and 64,429 people cannot deserve a full lesson in a seventeen-lesson topic. The space would be better spent on the larger archipelago.

**Reply.** ⚠️ Lakshadweep earns the lesson on four independent grounds, none of which is its size. ❗ **One**, it is India's only **atoll** system and therefore the only Indian demonstration of the reef form that Lessons 6 and 7 spend two lessons establishing. ❗ **Two**, it carries the **nested-area hierarchy**, which is the clearest available illustration that maritime extent follows dispersion rather than land area. ❗ **Three**, it is the archipelago in which India's **most severe measured bleaching** occurred — **84.6 per cent in 2023-24**, with three islands at about **98 per cent** — so Lessons 8, 9 and 11 all land here. ❗ **Four**, it is the test case for the **IIMP tier** of Lesson 15, because **no Lakshadweep island is among the eight ICRZ islands**, and the commonest legal error in the topic is to assume otherwise.

❓ **Residual, which this edition does not close.** ⚠️ **No official nested maritime series comparable to Lakshadweep's was retrieved for Andaman and Nicobar**, so the comparison above is deliberately incomplete and **no A&N figure is inferred**. ⚠️ ❓ **No cause is asserted for the specific west-lagoon, east-steep orientation**, since the source gives none. ⚠️ ❓ **The 3.40 ratio between Khullar's Lakshadweep area and the official one remains unreconciled (Lesson 13)**, and this lesson uses only the official figure.

### 8. Traps specific to this lesson

- ❌ ⚠️ **Calling Lakshadweep "fringing" or "atoll" without a scale.** ❗ **Atoll** at archipelago and tract scale; **fringing** on each island's seaward face (N-34).
- ❌ ⚠️ **Using 108.78 sq km or "25 islands".** ❗ Official land area **32 sq km**; official composition yields **36** (N-09).
- ❌ ⚠️ **Writing "Amendivi" or "Betra".** ❗ **Amindivi** and **Bitra**; Khullar's own figure prints AMINDIVI (N-11).
- ❌ ⚠️ **Confusing the Nine Degree Channel with the Ten Degree Channel**, or placing the **Eight Degree Channel** inside the main cluster.
- ❌ ⚠️ **Reproducing "12º 13″ North".** ❗ Read as **12° 13′ N**; the defect is disclosed (N-32).
- ❌ ⚠️ **Treating "uninhabited" as a physical fact.** ❗ **Bangaram is uninhabited and was enumerated with a population of 61 in 2011.**
- ❌ ⚠️ **Arguing UNCLOS, the territorial sea or the exclusive economic zone as law.** 🔁 `International-Relations/basic/04`.
- ❌ ⚠️ **Arguing Scheduled Tribe classification policy.** 🔁 `Social-Justice/basic/08` and `Polity/basic/Scheduled-and-Tribal-Areas`.
- ❌ ⚠️ **Asserting that Lakshadweep sits on a subsided volcanic island.** ❗ **The form fits Darwin's terminal state; the foundation was not verified here.**

### UPSC application

- **Syllabus:** ⚠️ Mapped clause, **first limb** — *geographical features and their location*; and the **second limb** through the archipelago's exposure to reef change, which Lessons 9 and 11 supply.
- **Verified PYQ:** ⚠️ **No owned demand names Lakshadweep.** ❗ **O1's key is unavailable locally and none is inferred.** ⚠️ **Supports O2 directly**, since the 84.6 per cent figure is an archipelago-level measurement for **this** archipelago and the sediment-and-lens consequence of Lesson 9 applies to **these** islands.
- **Traps:** ⚠️ The nine above. ❗ The scale trap and the ICRZ assumption, which Lesson 15 corrects, are the two that an examiner can detect immediately.
- **Answer use:** ❗ Lead with the **inversion**, because it is memorable, official and analytically loaded. ⚠️ *"Lakshadweep is India's smallest Union Territory by land — 32 sq km — and one of its largest maritime jurisdictions, with about 4,200 sq km of lagoon, 20,000 sq km of territorial waters and about 4 lakh sq km of economic zone, because maritime extent follows the dispersion of islands rather than their area; and every square kilometre of that land is carbonate its own reefs produced."*

Revision notes:

- 📰 **Official composition (Administration of Lakshadweep, retrieved this audit):** **12 atolls, 3 reefs, 5 submerged banks** = **10 inhabited islands + 17 uninhabited islands and attached islets + 4 newly formed islets + 5 submerged reefs = 36**. ❗ **Derived, not quoted bare.**
- ❌ **[N-34] SCALE:** **atoll archipelago** at archipelago and tract scale; ✅ Khullar's *"surrounded by **fringing reefs**"* at individual-island scale. ❗ **Both true; state the scale every time.**
- ✅ **[Khullar, printed 89] The 11° N rule:** north = **Amindivi** group; south = **Cannanore** group; *"in the extreme south is the **Minicoy** island."* ❌ **[N-11]** Khullar's text prints *"Amendivi"*; **his own Fig. 3.15 prints AMINDIVI**.
- ⚠️ **Channels:** **Nine Degree Channel** (Minicoy ↔ main group; ❗ not the A&N divider) · ✅ **Eight Degree Channel** (Minicoy ↔ Maldives, Majid 2.42; ❗ not within the main cluster).
- 📰 ❗ **NESTED-AREA HIERARCHY:** land **32 sq km** → lagoon about **4,200 sq km** (≈ **131 ×**) → territorial waters **20,000 sq km** (**625 ×**) → economic zone about **4 lakh sq km** (≈ **12,500 ×**).
- ❗ **The inversion:** India's **smallest** UT by land commands one of its **largest** maritime spaces. ⚠️ **Dispersion, not size, generates maritime extent.**
- 🔁 **UNCLOS, territorial sea and EEZ as law → `International-Relations/basic/04_Indo-Pacific-Indian-Ocean-and-Maritime-Security`.** ❗ The areas here are the **Administration's stated figures**, used as geography.
- ✅ **Relief and asymmetry (Khullar, 89):** *"not more than **five metre**"*; *"**flat**"*; hills, streams and valleys *"**conspicuous by their absence**"*; **lagoons west, slopes steeper east**. ❗ **No streams = no surface freshwater; the lens is everything (Lesson 9).**
- ⚠️ **Asymmetry mechanism:** sediment driven across the reef flat accumulates on the **sheltered** side; the exposed side stays steep. ❓ **No cause asserted for the specific west-east orientation.**
- 📰 **Settlement:** population **64,429 (2011)**; capital **Kavaratti**; **Bitra** smallest inhabited, **271 (2011)**; **Bangaram uninhabited yet enumerated with 61 (2011)** — ❗ *"inhabited" is a **definitional** category.
- ❌ **[N-32]** The page prints *"**12º 13″**"* with a **seconds mark where minutes is meant**; read as **12° 13′ N**. ❗ Also calls the same five features *"submerged banks"* and *"submerged reefs"* in consecutive sentences. ❗ Disclosed, not reproduced.
- 📰 **Language and economy:** Malayalam throughout, *"**except Minicoy where people speak Mahl which is written in Divehi script** and is spoken in **Maldives** also"* — ⚠️ **linguistic affinity follows sea distance, not administrative boundary**; fishing, coconut and coir, with ✅ Majid 2.42 adding *"coconut is the only major crop"*, *"fishing is the main occupation"* and **Minicoy**'s *"light house and a weather observatory"*.
- 📰 **Stated once, then routed:** *"The entire indigenous population has been classified as **Scheduled Tribes**"* under the **1956** modification orders; *"There are **no Scheduled Castes** in this Union Territory."* 🔁 `Social-Justice/basic/08` and `Polity/basic/Scheduled-and-Tribal-Areas`.
- ❗ **Why this archipelago matters to the topic:** India's **only atoll system** · the **nested-area inversion** · India's **most severe measured bleaching** (**84.6 per cent**, 04 DEC 2024; **about 98 per cent** on three islands, 16.12.2024) · and the **IIMP test case** of Lesson 15, since **no Lakshadweep island is among the eight ICRZ islands**.
- 🔑 **Mnemonic for the hierarchy — "L-L-T-E": Land 32 · Lagoon 4,200 · Territorial 20,000 · EEZ 4 lakh.**

### Concept check

**Question:** A candidate writes: "Lakshadweep, India's smallest Union Territory, consists of 36 coral islands covering 32 sq km; being fringing reefs, they are protected under the ICRZ 200-metre zone." Identify each defect and give the corrected statement, including the scale discipline.

**Model answer:** There are four defects. **First**, "36 coral islands" misstates what the 36 counts. The Administration of Lakshadweep describes the territory as **12 atolls, three reefs and five submerged banks**, composed of 10 inhabited islands, 17 uninhabited islands and attached islets, 4 newly formed islets and 5 submerged reefs — a composition that sums to 36. Only some of those 36 are islands at all; five are submerged reefs. The figure is a **composition total**, not an island count, and it must be derived rather than quoted. **Second**, "being fringing reefs" collapses two scales. At archipelago and reef-tract scale Lakshadweep is an **atoll** system, which is why its Administration counts atolls; at individual-island scale Khullar correctly describes the islands as *"surrounded by fringing reefs"*, since each island on an atoll rim has its own shore-attached reef on its seaward face. Both statements are true and the scale must be stated with the term. **Third**, the ICRZ claim is wrong, and it is the commonest legal error in this topic. The Island Coastal Regulation Zone Notification, 2019 declares ICRZ for **eight named bigger oceanic islands in Andaman and Nicobar only**, classified by geographical area, with the 200-metre landward zone applying to Group-I islands exceeding 1,000 sq km. **No Lakshadweep island is among the eight.** All other islands, including the whole of Lakshadweep, are covered by the notification but are **managed through Integrated Island Management Plans**, not through the ICRZ categories. **Fourth**, "smallest Union Territory" is true only by land and conceals the more interesting fact: against 32 sq km of land the Administration records about 4,200 sq km of lagoon, 20,000 sq km of territorial waters and about 4 lakh sq km of economic zone. The corrected statement: *"Lakshadweep is an atoll archipelago of 12 atolls, three reefs and five submerged banks, with 32 sq km of land on which ten islands are inhabited; each island carries fringing reef on its seaward face; and although the territory is covered by the ICRZ Notification 2019, it is managed through Integrated Island Management Plans rather than through the ICRZ categories, none of whose eight named islands lies in Lakshadweep."*

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not infer a legal regime from a physical similarity. Lakshadweep's islands are coastal, small and reef-fringed, which makes it intuitive to assume they fall under the same zonation as the Andaman islands; but the notification's categories are triggered by **named islands and geographical-area thresholds**, and Lakshadweep appears in neither. Physical likeness is not jurisdictional equivalence.

**Original Mains practice [15 marks]:** Account for the geography of Lakshadweep, and explain why its land area is the least informative statistic about it. *(Account for — answer in about 250 words.)*

**Model (about 250 words):** Lakshadweep is India's only atoll system: an oceanic, coral archipelago whose land is the comminuted product of its own reefs. Its Administration records **12 atolls, three reefs and five submerged banks**, with ten inhabited islands and a land area of **32 sq km**. Khullar describes the islands as rising *"not more than five metre above sea level"*, with *"flat"* topography in which *"hills, streams, valleys, etc. are conspicuous by their absence"*, and with *"shallow lagoons ... on their western side"* against *"steeper"* eastern slopes — an asymmetry that records where reef-derived sediment accumulates. He also supplies the internal division: islands north of **11° N** form the Amindivi group, those south the Cannanore group, with **Minicoy** beyond the Nine Degree Channel and the Eight Degree Channel separating it from the Maldives. Land area is the least informative statistic because the territory's significant magnitudes are maritime and nested: about **4,200 sq km** of lagoon, **20,000 sq km** of territorial waters and about **4 lakh sq km** of economic zone — roughly 131, 625 and 12,500 times the land respectively. Maritime extent follows the **dispersion** of islands, not their area, so the smallest Union Territory by land is among the largest by sea, administered from a population of 64,429 recorded in 2011. The absence of surface drainage makes the freshwater lens the only indigenous water source, and the 84.6 per cent bleaching recorded for 2023-24 therefore bears on sediment supply and habitability, not merely on ecology.

**Rubric (quantified, 15 marks):** **4** for atoll geography stated with the official composition and the correct genetic class, and with the Darwin connection limited to morphological fit; **3** for the internal division at 11° N with the Amindivi and Cannanore groups correctly placed, Minicoy identified as the southern outlier, and the Nine Degree and Eight Degree Channels distinguished; **5** for the nested-area hierarchy given completely with all four magnitudes and at least one ratio, and with the dispersion-not-size explanation supplied; **3** for the asymmetry detail reproduced from its source and explained as a sediment-distribution outcome, together with the no-surface-water consequence. Deduct 3 for asserting that Lakshadweep falls under the ICRZ 200-metre zone; deduct 2 for using any reef-form term without a stated scale.
