⚠️ *M6 is a legal-zonation schematic, not a map to scale. Distances on the cross-section are indicative; island positions are at **M3**, Lesson 12, and **M4**, Lesson 14.*

### 1. The intuition, before any terminology

⚠️ Most regulation is **administrative**: a rule applies because a government decided it should. ❗ **This one is geographical**, and that is why it belongs in a Geography edition rather than being routed out entirely.

⚠️ The instrument governing India's islands sorts them **by their geographical area**, and then assigns each class a **different statutory setback from the high tide line**. ⚠️ An island above a thousand square kilometres gets a two-hundred-metre regulated zone and a hundred-metre no-development zone. ⚠️ An island between a hundred and a thousand square kilometres gets half of each. ⚠️ Everything else — **including every island in Lakshadweep** — is covered by the notification but is managed through an entirely different device.

❗ **An island's legal setback is therefore a direct function of its area.** ⚠️ Change the area and you change the law. ❗ **That is a geographical classification rule expressed as a statute, and no previous topic in this lane has carried one.**

### 2. The instrument, identified exactly, and the correction that makes it necessary

📰 **Island Coastal Regulation Zone Notification, 2019 — `S.O. 1242(E)`**, Ministry of Environment, Forest and Climate Change, **New Delhi, the 8th March, 2019**, published in **THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY, PART II—Section 3—Sub-section (ii)**, No. 1106, Phalguna 17, 1940.

📰 Made under *"sub-section (1) and clause (v) of sub-section (2) of **section 3 of the Environment (Protection) Act, 1986**"*, and expressly *"**in supersession of Island Protection Zone Notification 2011, vide number S.O.20(E), dated the 6th January, 2011**"*, on the report of the committee chaired by **Dr. Shailesh Nayak**.

❌ ❗ **The correction (N-01), stated plainly because the error is inherited and widespread.**

| ⚠️ The inherited position | ✅ The correct position |
|---|---|
| ⚠️ *"Andaman and Nicobar and Lakshadweep are governed by the **Island Protection Zone Notification, 2011**."* | ✅ **IPZ 2011 — `S.O. 20(E)` of 6 January 2011 — was superseded on 8 March 2019 by the Island Coastal Regulation Zone (ICRZ) Notification, 2019, `S.O. 1242(E)`.** |
| ⚠️ ✅ C2 line 70: the Great Nicobar governance angle is *"**CRZ** clearances"* | ✅ **For Great Nicobar the instrument is ICRZ 2019**, and PIB's own Great Nicobar documents of 1 May 2026 cite the **ICRZ Notification, 2019** |

❗ **Note the gazette classification, because it is the cleanest way to keep the two instruments apart.** ⚠️ **CRZ 2019 is a `G.S.R.` published in Part II, Section 3, Sub-section (i).** ⚠️ **ICRZ 2019 is an `S.O.` published in Part II, Section 3, Sub-section (ii).** ❗ **Different instruments, different sub-sections, different territorial scope.**

❗ ⚠️ **And a disclosure that must travel with every citation of the instrument (U-08).** ⚠️ **The post-2019 amendment history of ICRZ 2019 was not retrieved in primary form in this audit.** ❗ **This edition states that the notification may have been amended since 2019 and cites no amendment number anywhere.**

### 3. Scope — the eight islands, named

📰 The notification declares as ICRZ *"the coastal stretches of the **eight bigger oceanic islands** in Andaman and Nicobar namely, **Middle Andaman, North Andaman, South Andaman, Great Nicobar, Baratang, Havelock, Little Andaman, Car Nicobar** Islands and the water area **up to territorial water limits** of the country"*.

⚠️ **Three observations, each of which is examinable.**

1. ❗ **All eight are in Andaman and Nicobar.** ⚠️ **No Lakshadweep island appears**, and §5 below is the consequence.
2. ⚠️ **The declaration extends seaward to territorial water limits**, so the instrument is not confined to a land strip.
3. ⚠️ **The phrase is *"bigger oceanic islands"***, which is the gazette's own language and signals that **size is the organising criterion** before any category is reached.

### 4. The area-threshold architecture — the topic's best legal-geographic payload

📰 From the notification:

| 📰 Class | 📰 Area criterion | 📰 Islands | 📰 ICRZ landward extent | 📰 No Development Zone | 📰 NDZ for eco-tourism |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 📰 **Group-I** | *"Islands with geographical areas **>1000 sq.km**"* | **South Andaman, Middle Andaman, North Andaman, Great Nicobar** | **HTL to 200 m** | **100 m from HTL** | **50 m** |
| 📰 **Group-II** | *"**>100 sq.km but < 1000 sq.km**"* | **Baratang, Little Andaman, Havelock, Car Nicobar** | **HTL to 100 m** | **50 m from HTL** | **20 m** |

❗ **Four quantities, two classes, one criterion — and the criterion is geographical area.** ⚠️ **This is the payload.** ⚠️ A candidate who can state that **Great Nicobar is a Group-I island because its geographical area exceeds 1,000 sq km, and that this is why its statutory setback is 200 metres with a 100-metre no-development zone**, has made a legal, a geographical and a quantitative statement in one sentence — and has also supplied the only verified statement about Great Nicobar's area that this edition permits (U-06).

📰 **Both NDZ provisions carry an express proviso**: *"the Andaman and Nicobar administration shall ensure that the concerns of the **fishing community** are fully protected"*.

📰 **Along tidal influenced water bodies**, the ICRZ and the NDZ run **HTL to 20 metres or the width of the creek, whichever is less**, with the tidal limit fixed by a **salinity of five parts per thousand measured during the driest period of the year**; and **until ICRZ Plans are approved**, *"the limit of **100 meters** or width of the creek whichever is less, shall continue to apply"*.

📰 ❗ **And one exemption that matters directly at Lesson 16**: *"**The NDZ shall not be applicable in such areas falling within notified Port limits.**"*

### 5. The two-tier architecture, and the Lakshadweep consequence

📰 **Paragraph 6 of the notification, "Areas requiring special consideration: Small Islands of Andaman Islands and Nicobar and Lakshadweep"**: *"(i) **All the smaller Islands** in Andaman and Nicobar and Lakshadweep, **other than those listed under the ICRZ categories, shall also be covered** under this Notification. (ii) These smaller islands shall be **managed through the respective Integrated Island Management Plans (IIMP)**"*, formulated by the respective Union Territory administration.

❗ **Therefore the governance of India's islands is a two-tier system, and almost every Indian island is in the second tier.**

| ⚠️ Tier | ⚠️ Who is in it | ⚠️ How it is governed |
|---|---|---|
| ⚠️ **Tier 1 — the ICRZ categories** | ⚠️ **Exactly eight islands**, all in Andaman and Nicobar, classified by area into Group-I and Group-II | ⚠️ ICRZ categories IA, IB, II, III, IVA, IVB, with the 200 m / 100 m landward extents and the 100 m / 50 m NDZs |
| ⚠️ **Tier 2 — the IIMP tier** | ⚠️ **Every other island in Andaman and Nicobar, and the whole of Lakshadweep** | ⚠️ *"Covered under this Notification"*, but **managed through Integrated Island Management Plans** prepared by the Union Territory administration |

❗ ❌ **The consequence, which corrects the commonest legal error in this topic (R-09).** ⚠️ **The claim "Lakshadweep is subject to the ICRZ 200 m zone" is false.** ❗ **No Lakshadweep island is among the eight.** ❗ **The correct statement is that Lakshadweep is covered by the notification and is managed through Integrated Island Management Plans.**

📰 ⚠️ One further administrative fact: ICRZ Plans *"shall **not normally be revised before a period of five years**"*.

### 6. The four categories, carried as a reference architecture

❗ ⚠️ **This block is carried as a single reference table and is not narrated**, because enumeration is Advanced material while the **architecture** — ecologically sensitive, intertidal, urban, rural, water — is Core, since it is a **zonation logic**.

| 📰 Category | 📰 What it is | ⚠️ Why the category exists |
|---|---|---|
| 📰 **ICRZ-IA** | Ecologically sensitive areas and geomorphological features that maintain the integrity of the coast: mangroves, with a **20 m buffer where the mangrove area exceeds 1,000 square metres**; ❗ **corals and coral reefs**; sand dunes; biologically active mudflats; national parks, marine parks, sanctuaries, reserve forests, wildlife habitats and other protected areas **including Biosphere Reserves**; salt marshes; **turtle nesting grounds**; **horse shoe crab's habitat**; sea grass beds; seaweeds; nesting grounds of birds; areas or structures of archaeological importance and heritage sites — all to be mapped by **NCSCM** under Annexure-I and integrated in the ICRZ Plans | ⚠️ The **most protected** class, defined by **ecological and geomorphological function** rather than by land use |
| 📰 **ICRZ-IB** | The **intertidal zone** between the Low Tide Line and the High Tide Line | ⚠️ A **physically defined** strip |
| 📰 **ICRZ-II** | Developed land within municipal or legally designated urban limits, *"substantially built-up with a ratio of built up plots to that of total plots being **more than 50 per cent**"* and provided with drainage, approach roads, water supply and sewerage | ⚠️ A **land-use and infrastructure** test with a numerical threshold |
| 📰 **ICRZ-III** | Relatively undisturbed and **rural** land | ⚠️ The residual land class |
| 📰 **ICRZ-IVA** | Water area from the **Low Tide Line to twelve nautical miles** seaward | ⚠️ The **marine** limb |
| 📰 **ICRZ-IVB** | Bank-to-bank water area of **tidal influenced water bodies** | ⚠️ The **estuarine** limb |

❗ ⚠️ **Corals are written into the most protected category**, which is the single most important fact in the table for this topic. 📰 It is independently corroborated by **PIB Release ID 2080596, MoES, 04 DEC 2024**: *"**Corals and coral reefs are classified as CRZ-IA area** under the Coastal Regulation Zone Notification 2011 and 2019."*

❌ ❗ **Two corrections on that corroboration (N-02).** ⚠️ **The PIB formulation is quoted as written.** ❗ **But for island reefs the instrument is ICRZ 2019 and the category is ICRZ-IA**, not CRZ-IA, because **CRZ 2019 governs the mainland coast and these islands are governed by ICRZ 2019**. ⚠️ **Two MoEFCC parliamentary answers — LS Q.4311 of 13.12.2019 and LS Q.247 of 02.02.2026 — likewise cite "the Coastal Regulation Zone (CRZ) Notification, 2019" while answering about Lakshadweep and Andaman and Nicobar.** ❗ **This edition names ICRZ 2019 as the island instrument and discloses that official replies cite CRZ 2019; it does not pretend the replies say otherwise.**

❌ ❗ **A drafting defect in the gazette itself, disclosed once and not reproduced (N-31).** ⚠️ **The notification prints *"such area shall also constitute CRZ –I A"* inside its ICRZ-IA clause, and heads the next clause *"The CCRZ-IB"*.** ❗ **Both are read as ICRZ-IA and ICRZ-IB.** ❗ **The defective strings are disclosed here and are not reproduced anywhere else in this edition.**

### 7. The High Tide Line, and the Topic 10 boundary

📰 **ICRZ 2019, Explanation**: *"'**High Tide Line**' means the line on the land upto which the highest water line reaches during the **spring tide**, as demarcated by the **National Centre for Sustainable Coastal Management (NCSCM), Chennai** in accordance with the laid down procedures."*

⚠️ **Three things follow and all three matter.**

1. ⚠️ **The HTL is a demarcated line, not a natural object.** ⚠️ It is defined by a **tidal condition** (the spring tide) and produced by a **named institution** following laid-down procedures. ❗ Every ICRZ distance in this lesson is measured **from that line**, so the zonation inherits whatever convention the demarcation uses.
2. ⚠️ **It is the same regulatory line that NCSCM also uses in mapping reefs**, which is why Lesson 10's 1,439 sq km figure is *"as per the Coastal Regulation Zone Notification (CRZ), 2011 and 2019"* — ❗ **the institution, the line and the instrument are the same system**, and that is why the mapped extent is not an estimate.
3. 🔁 ❗ **The HTL is *not* the measurement line used for coastline length.** ❗ **Topic 11 carries the island half of that distinction and teaches no coastline length at all.** 🔁 **The coastline-measurement series routes in its entirety to `Geography/basic/10_Coastal-Landforms` and `Geography/advanced/10_India-Coast-and-CRZ`.**

🔁 ❗ **The other route-outs this lesson must perform.** ⚠️ **CRZ 2019 as the mainland instrument, the hazard line, Coastal Zone Management Plans and mangrove buffer policy** route to **`Geography/basic/10_Coastal-Landforms`** and **`Geography/advanced/10_India-Coast-and-CRZ`**, with the ecological content to **`Environment-and-Ecology/basic/24_Coastal-and-Marine-Ecology-CRZ-Blue-Economy`**. ⚠️ **Biosphere Reserve designation and protected-area law** — which ICRZ-IA names but does not define — route to **`Environment-and-Ecology/basic/07_Biosphere-Reserves-and-Ramsar-Sites`**. ❗ **ICRZ clearance procedure, appraisal machinery and authorities are excluded entirely**, because environmental-regulatory design is not claimed by Geography.

### 8. 🇮🇳 India hook — the whole lesson, and the sentence it buys

🇮🇳 ⚠️ This lesson is entirely Indian. ❗ What it buys is the sentence that makes Lesson 16 possible:

> ⚠️ *"India's islands are governed not by the mainland's CRZ 2019 but by the **Island Coastal Regulation Zone Notification, 2019, `S.O. 1242(E)` of 8 March 2019**, which **superseded** the Island Protection Zone Notification of 2011 and classifies **eight named Andaman and Nicobar islands by geographical area**: Group-I above **1,000 sq km** — South Andaman, Middle Andaman, North Andaman and **Great Nicobar** — with ICRZ from the high tide line to **200 m** and a **100 m** no-development zone, and Group-II between **100 and 1,000 sq km** with **100 m** and **50 m** respectively; **every other island, including the whole of Lakshadweep, is covered by the notification but managed through Integrated Island Management Plans**."*

❗ **That paragraph does four things**: it identifies the instrument correctly with its gazette citation, it states a **geographical** classification rule, it places **Great Nicobar** in Group-I — which is the only verified statement about that island's area available here — and it forecloses the commonest error about Lakshadweep.

### 9. Comparison, objection and the residual

| ⚠️ | ✅ **CRZ 2019** | ✅ **ICRZ 2019** |
|---|---|---|
| ⚠️ Gazette form | ⚠️ A **G.S.R.**, Part II—Sec. 3(i) | 📰 An **S.O.**, Part II—**Sec. 3(ii)**; `S.O. 1242(E)` |
| ⚠️ Date | ⚠️ 2019 | 📰 **8 March 2019** |
| ⚠️ Territorial scope | ⚠️ The **mainland** coast | 📰 **Eight named Andaman and Nicobar islands**, plus coverage of all other A&N and Lakshadweep islands through IIMPs |
| ⚠️ Classification basis | ⚠️ Land-use and ecological categories | 📰 **Island geographical area first**, then the same category families |
| ⚠️ Supersedes | ⚠️ Earlier CRZ notifications | 📰 **IPZ 2011, `S.O. 20(E)` of 6 January 2011** |
| ⚠️ Committee | ⚠️ Not carried here | 📰 **Dr. Shailesh Nayak Committee** |
| ⚠️ Owner | 🔁 `Geography/basic/10` and `advanced/10` | ⚠️ **This lesson** |

**Strongest objection.** ⚠️ A gazette notification is environmental law. A physical-geography edition that teaches setback distances has crossed into Environment and Ecology and should have routed the whole of it.

**Reply.** ⚠️ The objection would be right about almost any other notification, and this edition routes almost everything about this one. ❗ **What is kept is the part that is irreducibly geographical: the classification of islands by area, and the consequence that a statutory distance varies with that area.** ⚠️ That is a **spatial classification rule** of the same species as a size-based settlement hierarchy or a basin-order scheme, and it is the only instance in this lane where a legal threshold is a **geographical variable**. ❗ What is routed, by exact path, is the procedure, the appraisal machinery, the authorities, the ecological content of the categories, the designation law and the mainland instrument. ⚠️ Moreover, the instrument is **load-bearing for Lesson 16**: the project there sits on a **Group-I** island, and the NDZ's **port-limit exemption** bears directly on it.

❓ **Residual, which this edition does not close.** ⚠️ **The post-2019 amendment history was not retrieved (U-08)** and **no amendment number is cited anywhere**. ⚠️ ❓ **Great Nicobar's actual geographical area is not stated anywhere in this edition (U-06)**; the only verified statement is the notification's own *"more than 1,000 sq.km"*, and the attempt at Lesson 13 to compute an area from Khullar's dimensions was expressly refused. ⚠️ ❓ **No ICRZ Plan or Integrated Island Management Plan for any island was retrieved**, so this edition says what the instrument requires and **nothing about what any plan contains**.

### 10. Traps specific to this lesson

- ❌ ⚠️ **Naming the Island Protection Zone Notification, 2011 as the current instrument.** ❗ **Superseded 8 March 2019** by ICRZ 2019, `S.O. 1242(E)` (N-01).
- ❌ ⚠️ **Calling the Great Nicobar instrument "CRZ".** ❗ It is **ICRZ 2019**; C2 line 70 is corrected, and PIB's own project documents cite ICRZ 2019.
- ❌ ⚠️ **Saying Lakshadweep is subject to the ICRZ 200 m zone.** ❗ **No Lakshadweep island is among the eight**; the whole territory is in the **IIMP tier**.
- ❌ ⚠️ **Mixing up the gazette classes.** ❗ **CRZ 2019 = G.S.R., Sec. 3(i); ICRZ 2019 = S.O., Sec. 3(ii).**
- ❌ ⚠️ **Giving the NDZ as a single figure.** ❗ **Group-I 100 m, Group-II 50 m**, reduced to **50 m and 20 m** for eco-tourism, and **not applicable within notified port limits**.
- ❌ ⚠️ **Reproducing the gazette's "CRZ –I A" or "CCRZ-IB" strings.** ❗ Drafting defects; read as **ICRZ-IA** and **ICRZ-IB** (N-31).
- ❌ ⚠️ **Citing an ICRZ amendment.** ❗ **The amendment history was not retrieved and no amendment number is cited (U-08).**
- ❌ ⚠️ **Teaching coastline length because the HTL is defined here.** 🔁 `Geography/basic/10_Coastal-Landforms`.

### UPSC application

- **Syllabus:** ⚠️ Mapped clause, **first limb** — *geographical features and their location*. ⚠️ A zonation keyed to island area is a classification **of geographical features by a geographical property**, which is why it sits inside the clause rather than outside it.
- **Verified PYQ:** ⚠️ **No owned demand is a regulation demand.** ❗ **O1's key is unavailable locally and none is inferred.** ⚠️ The lesson's examination value lies in **precision under pressure**: the instrument's name, date, gazette sub-section, supersession, two area thresholds, four distances and two tiers are all exactly checkable, and almost no answer will have them right.
- **Traps:** ⚠️ The eight above. ❗ The IPZ-as-current trap and the Lakshadweep-200-metre trap are the two most likely to appear.
- **Answer use:** ❗ When an island-governance question arises, **lead with the area rule, not with the category list**. ⚠️ *"Island coastal regulation in India is keyed to island area: the ICRZ Notification 2019 classifies eight named Andaman and Nicobar islands into Group-I above 1,000 sq km, with a 200-metre regulated zone and a 100-metre no-development zone, and Group-II between 100 and 1,000 sq km at half those distances, while every other island — including all of Lakshadweep — is covered by the notification but managed through Integrated Island Management Plans."*

Revision notes:

- 📰 ❗ **The instrument:** **ICRZ Notification, 2019 — `S.O. 1242(E)`**, MoEFCC, **8 March 2019**, **GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY, PART II—Sec. 3—Sub-sec. (ii)**, No. 1106, Phalguna 17, 1940; under **section 3 of the Environment (Protection) Act, 1986**; on the report of the **Dr. Shailesh Nayak** committee.
- ❌ **[N-01] SUPERSESSION:** *"in supersession of **Island Protection Zone Notification 2011**, vide number **S.O.20(E), dated the 6th January, 2011**."* ❗ **IPZ 2011 is not the current instrument.** ❗ C2 line 70's *"CRZ clearances"* for Great Nicobar is corrected to **ICRZ 2019**.
- ❗ **Gazette discipline:** **CRZ 2019 = G.S.R., Sec. 3(i)** (mainland). **ICRZ 2019 = S.O., Sec. 3(ii)** (islands).
- 📰 **Scope:** *"the coastal stretches of the **eight bigger oceanic islands** in Andaman and Nicobar namely, **Middle Andaman, North Andaman, South Andaman, Great Nicobar, Baratang, Havelock, Little Andaman, Car Nicobar** ... and the water area **up to territorial water limits**."*
- 📰 ❗ **THE AREA GATE:** **Group-I, *">1000 sq.km"*** — South Andaman, Middle Andaman, North Andaman, **Great Nicobar** — **ICRZ HTL to 200 m**, **NDZ 100 m** (**50 m** eco-tourism). **Group-II, *">100 sq.km but < 1000 sq.km"*** — Baratang, Little Andaman, Havelock, Car Nicobar — **ICRZ HTL to 100 m**, **NDZ 50 m** (**20 m** eco-tourism).
- ❗ **An island's statutory setback is a DIRECT FUNCTION OF ITS AREA** — the payload. 📰 **NDZ proviso:** the A&N administration *"shall ensure that the concerns of the **fishing community** are fully protected"*. 📰 ❗ *"**The NDZ shall not be applicable in such areas falling within notified Port limits.**"*
- 📰 **Tidal influenced water bodies:** ICRZ and NDZ **HTL to 20 m or the creek width, whichever is less**; tidal limit at **salinity five parts per thousand** in the **driest period**; until ICRZ Plans are approved, **100 m or creek width, whichever is less**, continues.
- 📰 **[D-09] THE TWO TIERS:** *"**All the smaller Islands** in Andaman and Nicobar and **Lakshadweep**, other than those listed under the ICRZ categories, **shall also be covered**"* and *"shall be **managed through the respective Integrated Island Management Plans (IIMP)**"*. ❗ **NO LAKSHADWEEP ISLAND IS AMONG THE EIGHT.** ❗ *"Lakshadweep is subject to the ICRZ 200 m zone"* is **false**.
- 📰 **Categories:** **IA** ecologically sensitive — ❗ **corals and coral reefs**, mangroves with a **20 m buffer above 1,000 sq m**, dunes, mudflats, parks and **Biosphere Reserves**, salt marshes, **turtle nesting grounds**, **horse shoe crab's habitat**, sea grass, seaweed, bird nesting grounds, archaeological and heritage sites — mapped by **NCSCM** under Annexure-I · **IB** intertidal LTL-HTL · **II** urban, *"built up plots ... **more than 50 per cent**"* with drainage, roads, water and sewerage · **III** rural · **IVA** LTL to **twelve nautical miles** · **IVB** tidal water bodies bank to bank. 📰 Plans *"shall not normally be revised before **five years**"*.
- 📰 **Corroboration and its correction [N-02]:** PIB 2080596 (**04 DEC 2024**) says *"Corals and coral reefs are classified as **CRZ-IA** area under the Coastal Regulation Zone Notification 2011 and 2019"*; ❗ **for island reefs read ICRZ-IA**. ⚠️ MoEFCC replies of **13.12.2019** and **02.02.2026** also cite CRZ 2019 while answering about the islands; **disclosed, not pretended away**.
- ❌ **[N-31]** The gazette itself prints *"CRZ –I A"* and *"The CCRZ-IB"*. ❗ **Drafting defects; read as ICRZ-IA and ICRZ-IB; strings not reproduced.**
- 📰 **HTL:** *"the line on the land upto which the highest water line reaches during the **spring tide**, as demarcated by the **National Centre for Sustainable Coastal Management (NCSCM), Chennai**."* ❗ A **demarcated** line, not a natural object. ❗ **Not the coastline-measurement line** — 🔁 `Geography/basic/10_Coastal-Landforms`.
- ❗ **[U-08] Post-2019 amendment history NOT retrieved; no amendment number cited.** ❗ **[U-06] Great Nicobar's area is stated only as *">1000 sq.km"*.**
- 🔁 **Routes:** mainland CRZ 2019, hazard line, CZMP → `Geography/basic/10` and `advanced/10`; ecological content → `Environment-and-Ecology/basic/24`; Biosphere Reserve designation → `Environment-and-Ecology/basic/07`. ❗ **Clearance procedure and appraisal machinery: excluded entirely.**
- 🔑 **Mnemonic for the gate — "Thousand, two hundred, hundred":** above **1,000 sq km** → **200 m** ICRZ → **100 m** NDZ; the next class halves each.

### Concept check

**Question:** A proposal is made to build a resort 120 metres from the high tide line on Havelock Island, and a second resort 120 metres from the high tide line on Kavaratti in Lakshadweep. Set out exactly which instrument and which provisions apply in each case, and what further information you would need.

**Model answer:** The two cases fall under **different tiers of the same notification**, and that is the whole answer. The governing instrument in both cases is the **Island Coastal Regulation Zone Notification, 2019, `S.O. 1242(E)` of 8 March 2019**, which superseded the Island Protection Zone Notification 2011, `S.O. 20(E)` of 6 January 2011. **CRZ 2019 does not govern either site**, since it is a separate instrument — a G.S.R. in Part II, Section 3, Sub-section (i) — applying to the mainland coast. **Havelock** is one of the **eight bigger oceanic islands** named in the notification and is classified **Group-II**, for islands *">100 sq.km but < 1000 sq.km"*. Its ICRZ therefore runs from the high tide line to **100 metres** landward, and its No Development Zone is **50 metres from the HTL**, reduced to **20 metres** for eco-tourism activities, with the express proviso that the administration must ensure the concerns of the fishing community are fully protected. A site at 120 metres lies **beyond the 100-metre ICRZ**, so the ICRZ categories do not reach it, though the proposal would still have to satisfy whatever else applies outside the zone. **Kavaratti** is **not among the eight**. No Lakshadweep island is. Under paragraph 6 of the notification, all smaller islands in Andaman and Nicobar **and Lakshadweep** other than those listed under the ICRZ categories are nonetheless **covered by the notification** and are **managed through the respective Integrated Island Management Plan** prepared by the Union Territory administration. The applicable control at Kavaratti is therefore the IIMP, not a 200-metre or 100-metre ICRZ line. Three further facts would be needed. For Havelock: whether the site lies within **notified Port limits**, since the NDZ does not apply there; whether it lies along a **tidal influenced water body**, where the zone is 20 metres or the creek width, whichever is less, and 100 metres applies until ICRZ Plans are approved; and whether any **ICRZ-IA** feature — corals, turtle nesting grounds, mangroves above 1,000 square metres — has been mapped by NCSCM at the site. For Kavaratti: the content and approval status of the relevant IIMP, which this edition has not seen.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not assume that because both islands are small, tropical and reef-fringed, the same numerical setback applies to both. The notification's distances attach to **named islands classified by geographical area**, and an island outside that list receives no ICRZ distance at all — it receives a management plan. Transferring a setback figure from one island to another is the single most common legal error in this topic.

**Original Mains practice [20 marks]:** "The Island Coastal Regulation Zone Notification, 2019 is a geographical instrument as much as a regulatory one." Examine this proposition. *(Examine — answer in about 250 words.)*

**Model (about 250 words):** The proposition holds because the notification's primary classification is a **geographical variable**. ICRZ 2019, `S.O. 1242(E)` of 8 March 2019, published in Part II, Section 3, Sub-section (ii) of the Extraordinary Gazette, supersedes the Island Protection Zone Notification 2011 and declares ICRZ for **eight bigger oceanic islands** in Andaman and Nicobar. It then sorts them by **geographical area**. **Group-I**, *">1000 sq.km"* — South Andaman, Middle Andaman, North Andaman and Great Nicobar — receives an ICRZ from the high tide line to **200 metres** and a No Development Zone of **100 metres**, reduced to 50 metres for eco-tourism. **Group-II**, *">100 sq.km but < 1000 sq.km"* — Baratang, Little Andaman, Havelock and Car Nicobar — receives **100 metres** and **50 metres**, reduced to 20 metres. An island's statutory setback is thus a direct function of its area. The geography continues downward. The zone is measured from the **high tide line**, defined by the spring tide and demarcated by NCSCM. ICRZ-IA is defined by **ecological and geomorphological function** and expressly includes corals and coral reefs. ICRZ-IVA extends to **twelve nautical miles**. Along tidal water bodies the zone is 20 metres or the creek width, with the tidal limit set by a **salinity of five parts per thousand** in the driest period. Finally, the architecture is two-tier: all other islands, including the whole of Lakshadweep, are covered but **managed through Integrated Island Management Plans**. The regulatory content is real, but every trigger in it is spatial.

**Rubric (quantified, 20 marks):** **7** for the area-threshold architecture given completely and correctly — both class definitions with their exact wording, all eight islands assigned, and both ICRZ landward extents — and for drawing the conclusion that the setback is a function of area; **5** for NDZ conditionality, covering both base distances, both eco-tourism reductions, the fishing-community proviso and the notified-port-limits exemption; **4** for the IIMP tier stated with its paragraph's substance and the Lakshadweep consequence drawn explicitly; **3** for the CRZ-versus-ICRZ distinction, including the gazette forms and the disclosure that official replies cite CRZ 2019 for island reefs; **1** for supersession correctness, naming `S.O. 20(E)` of 6 January 2011 and the 8 March 2019 date. Deduct 4 for stating that Lakshadweep falls under an ICRZ setback distance; deduct 2 for citing any ICRZ amendment number.

⚠️ *The figure is a schematic footprint overlay with an audit sidebar, not a map to scale. Island outline, positions and proportions are indicative; the island's actual area is not stated anywhere in this edition (U-06).*

### 1. The intuition, before any terminology

⚠️ A mega-project is usually argued about rather than read. ❗ **This lesson does something different: it reads the project's own factsheets as a geographer would read any quantitative source — by checking whether the numbers close.**

⚠️ Two first-party documents were published by **PIB Research on the same day, 1 May 2026**. ⚠️ **Four of their published sums close exactly.** ⚠️ **Four do not.** ⚠️ **One of their most-quoted statistics has the wrong denominator attached to it in one document and the right one in the other.** ⚠️ **And one component is assigned the entire footprint.**

❗ **Showing which sums close and which do not is better teaching than either praising or attacking the project**, and it is also the only treatment available to a Geography edition, which owns the **setting** — location, depth, footprint, land-use change, reef and seismic exposure, and the arithmetic — and routes everything else.

### 2. The locational appraisal, which is the geography

📰 **PIB Research backgrounder, 1 May 2026:**

| 📰 Locational fact | 📰 Value | ⚠️ Why it is geographical |
|---|---|---|
| 📰 Proximity to the **East-West international shipping route** | **about 40 nautical miles** | ⚠️ Position relative to a global traffic lane is the site's primary asset |
| 📰 **Natural water depth** at Galathea Bay | **over 20 metres** | ⚠️ A **physical site quality**: depth that does not have to be created |
| 📰 **International Container Transhipment Terminal** | **14.2 million TEU** | ⚠️ Stated as a planned capacity, not as a site quality |
| 📰 **Greenfield International Airport** | **4,000 Peak Hour Passengers**; existing Sri Vijaya Puram traffic *"approximately **1.8 million passengers annually**"*; expected to open at *"at least **1 million**"* and grow to *"approximately **10 million** passengers per year"* | ⚠️ Capacity figures, dated to the factsheet |
| 📰 **Power plant** | **450 MVA gas and solar**, replacing diesel generating sets as the islands' major energy source | ⚠️ An energy-geography statement about an isolated system |
| 📰 Stated purpose | Reducing dependence on **Colombo, Singapore and Klang** | 🔁 **Transshipment economics is routed** |

✅ **Khullar, printed folio 89**, adds the independent locational fact: Great Nicobar is **147 km from Sumatra**, across the **Great Channel**. ⚠️ **Indira Point**, on the same island, is the **southernmost point of Indian territory** — ❗ **not Indira Col**, and **Kanyakumari is mainland India's southern tip**.

📰 **ICRZ 2019, `S.O. 1242(E)`**, supplies the legal-geographic fact that Lesson 15 established: **Great Nicobar is a Group-I island**, *"with geographical areas **>1000 sq.km**"*, and therefore carries an **ICRZ from HTL to 200 m** with a **100-metre No Development Zone**. ❗ **And the NDZ *"shall not be applicable in such areas falling within notified Port limits"*** — an exemption that bears directly on a port project and is stated here as a fact of the instrument, not as an evaluation of it.

❗ ⚠️ **The two-sentence locational appraisal an answer actually needs:** *"Galathea Bay's site qualities are a natural depth of over 20 metres and a position about 40 nautical miles from the East-West international shipping route, on an island whose southern tip is India's southernmost territory and which lies 147 km from Sumatra across the Great Channel. Those are physical and positional facts; the capacity, market-share and strategic arguments built on them belong to other owners."*

### 3. The arithmetic that closes

⚠️ **Four published sums were re-summed in this audit and close exactly.** ❗ They are stated here **with the closure shown**, because demonstrating that a source's arithmetic is internally consistent is as much a part of quantitative hygiene as catching an error.

| ⚠️ Group | 📰 Components | ⚠️ Sum | ⚠️ Published total | ⚠️ Residual |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ⚠️ **Phasing** | **Phase I 2025-35: 72.12** · **Phase II 2036-41: 45.27** · **Phase III 2042-47: 48.71** sq km | **166.10** | **166.10 sq km** | ⚠️ **Zero** |
| ⚠️ **Land class** | **35.35 revenue land** + **130.75 forest land** sq km | **166.10** | **166.10 sq km** | ⚠️ **Zero** |
| ⚠️ **Tree felling** | **2.79 + 3.41 + 0.91** lakh | **7.11 lakh** | **7.11 lakh trees** | ⚠️ **Zero** |
| ⚠️ **Tribal reserve** | **84.10 overlap** − **11.032** already revenue land since **1972** | **73.068** | **73.07 de-notified** | ⚠️ **Rounding only** |

📰 Two further figures sit with these: **7.11 lakh trees of an estimated 18.65 lakh standing in the diverted area — 38.1 per cent**, computed here from the two published figures; and **Phase I involves 40.01 sq km of tribal area**.

📰 The FAQ states that **166.1 sq km is *"approximately 2 per cent of the total area of Andaman and Nicobar Islands"***. ⚠️ **Re-computed here against the official 8,249 sq km: 2.014 per cent.** ❗ ⚠️ **But note what Lesson 13 established — the 8,249 total is agreed while its internal partition is not**, so a percentage of an archipelago total is itself a **counting-convention claim** and must be stated as one.

❌ ❗ **The rounding discipline (N-36).** ⚠️ **84.10 − 11.032 = 73.068**, of which the published **73.07** is the rounded form. ⚠️ The **net addition** is **76.98 re-notified − 73.068 = 3.912 sq km**, and ❗ **that closes exactly only on the unrounded value**: 76.98 − 73.07 would give 3.91. ❗ **This edition discloses the rounding whenever the components are shown and never "fixes" the published figures.**

### 4. The arithmetic that does not close

❗ ⚠️ **Four published groups fail to reconcile. Each is stated with both sides quoted, both dated to 1 May 2026, and no repair attempted.**

❌ ❗ **(a) The felling-and-green-zone partition (N-35).** 📰 **Felling area 49.86 sq km** plus **green zone 65.99 sq km** = **115.85 sq km**, against **130.75 sq km** of diverted forest. ⚠️ **A residual of 14.90 sq km is unaccounted for.** ❗ **This edition states the residual and does not assign it to any category.**

❌ ❗ **(b) The Phase-I diversion against the felling area (N-35).** 📰 The backgrounder's **Phase-I diversion of 48.65 sq km** sits beside a **felling area of 49.86 sq km** without reconciliation. ❗ **Both are stated; neither is adjusted.**

❌ ❗ **(c) Compensatory afforestation, stated two different ways on the same day (N-05).**

| 📰 Document, 1 May 2026 | 📰 What it says |
|---|---|
| 📰 **Backgrounder** | Compensatory afforestation is *"planned in **Haryana**, with **97.30 sq. km** of land identified to compensate for Phase I diversion of **48.65 sq. km** forest"* — ⚠️ a ratio of exactly **2.0** |
| 📰 **FAQ** | *"**A total of 24,750.93 ha** has been identified for CA: **1,414.95 ha** of non-forest land (equivalent to the diverted area) and over double that extent in degraded forest land. Of this, about **17,000 ha is in Haryana** and **6,320.10 ha is in Madhya Pradesh**."* |

⚠️ **Three defects follow mechanically and all three are stated:**

1. ⚠️ **97.30 sq km is 9,730 ha, not about 17,000 ha.**
2. ⚠️ **17,000 + 6,320.10 = 23,320.10 ha** against a stated total of **24,750.93 ha**, leaving **1,430.83 ha unaccounted**.
3. ⚠️ **1,414.95 ha is described as *"equivalent to the diverted area"***, but the diverted area is **130.75 sq km = 13,075 ha** — a factor of **9.24**.

❗ **The edition presents the project's land arithmetic as an *unreconciled official set*, never silently averages or repairs it, and dates every figure to 1 May 2026.** ❗ **It does not choose between the backgrounder and the FAQ, and it does not state that either is wrong**, because no document in the retrieved record adjudicates between them.

📰 ⚠️ The **legal basis for off-island afforestation** is stated once, as a fact: **MoEF&CC Guidelines dated 22.05.2019** under the **Forest (Conservation) Act, 1980**, exempting States and Union Territories with **over 75 per cent forest cover** from providing non-forest land locally. 🔁 **Compensatory-afforestation policy design and forest law route to `Environment-and-Ecology/basic/11_Forest-Types-and-Forest-Rights-Act` and `basic/24`.**

❌ ❗ **(d) The township that is the whole project (N-04).** 📰 The backgrounder states *"A new township spanning **16,610 hectares**"* and, elsewhere, *"It covers a total area of **166.10 sq. km**"*. ⚠️ **16,610 ha is 166.10 sq km exactly.** ❗ **The township cannot simultaneously be one of four components and be the whole.** ❗ **This edition cites 166.10 sq km as the project footprint and does not repeat the township figure as a component area anywhere.**

### 5. The denominator trap — the topic's highest-value quantitative lesson

❌ ❗ **(N-03).** 📰 The backgrounder's **Key Takeaways** say *"diversion of **1.82% of island forest cover**"*. 📰 The **FAQ of the same date** says what is actually meant: *"**130.75 sq. km** of forest area is proposed to be diverted for the project which is only approximately **1.82% of the total forest area of A & N Islands**."*

⚠️ **The implied denominator is 130.75 ÷ 0.0182 ≈ 7,184 sq km**, which is the **recorded forest area of the whole Union Territory**, not of Great Nicobar island.

❗ ⚠️ **A disclosure that must travel with that number (U-03).** ⚠️ **The India State of Forest Report figure for Andaman and Nicobar's recorded forest area was not retrieved in primary form in this audit.** ❗ **The approximately 7,184 sq km is reported as *implied by dividing 130.75 by 0.0182*, and is never cited as an ISFR figure.**

⚠️ **Measured against Great Nicobar island itself, the diversion is of the order of 12 per cent**, not the **1.82 per cent** computed against the **Andaman and Nicobar total forest area** — ❗ and that order-of-magnitude statement is as far as this edition goes, because **the island's area is not stated anywhere in this edition (U-06)** and **no area may be computed from Khullar's "50 × 25 km" (N-29)**.

❗ **The rule, absolute: any sentence in this edition that uses 1.82 per cent names its denominator.** ❗ **This is the topic's highest-value quantitative trap**, because the figure is universally repeated in its wrong form and the correct denominator is available in the project's own companion document.

### 6. The clearance, oversight and hazard frame — stated once and routed immediately

⚠️ ❗ **This block is deliberately a single enumeration. Nothing in it is explained, because none of it is Geography's to explain.**

📰 From both PIB documents of **1 May 2026**: Prior Environmental Clearance under the **EIA Notification, 2006** and the **ICRZ Notification, 2019**, carrying **42 specific conditions** covering air, water, noise, waste, marine ecology, human health and disaster management, with an Environment Management Plan. 📰 Appraising institutions named: **ZSI, SACON, WII, IISc**, with **IITs, NIOT, NCCR and NIO** named in the FAQ. 📰 **Three independent Monitoring Committees** — pollution; biodiversity; welfare of the **Shompen** and **Nicobarese** — plus an **Overarching Committee chaired by the Chief Secretary, A&N Administration**. 📰 Mitigation expressly includes *"**coral protection and translocation**"*. 📰 The island *"lies in a **seismically sensitive and cyclone-prone** region"*, and the risk assessment covers **tsunamis, earthquakes, cyclones** and anthropogenic hazards. 📰 The FAQ quotes the **NGT order of 3 April 2023**, including its own notice of media reports on *"**string of pearls**"*, *"**Act East**"* and poaching from Myanmar involving *"destruction of corals, killing of sharks"*. 📰 The FAQ also records a **NOC from the Ministry of Tribal Affairs**, an **Empowered Committee**, and the populations **Shompen about 237** and **Nicobarese about 1,094**, with the settlements **New Chingen** and **Rajiv Nagar** named. 📰 **Phase I involves 40.01 sq km of tribal area.**

🔁 ❗ **And now the routing, by exact path, in the lesson where every temptation arises.**

| ⚠️ Element named above | 🔁 Owner |
|---|---|
| ⚠️ **Shompen and Nicobarese policy, PVTG criteria, Forest Rights Act, Article 338A(9), Scheduled Areas, consent and the Tribal Affairs NOC** | 🔁 **`Social-Justice/basic/08_Scheduled-Tribes-PVTGs-and-Tribal-Welfare`** and **`Polity/basic/Scheduled-and-Tribal-Areas`** |
| ⚠️ **EIA procedure, clearance appraisal, expert committees, NGT adjudication, compensatory-afforestation policy design, coral translocation as technique, marine ecology, leatherback turtle conservation** | 🔁 **`Environment-and-Ecology/basic/24_Coastal-and-Marine-Ecology-CRZ-Blue-Economy`**, with forest law to **`Environment-and-Ecology/basic/11_Forest-Types-and-Forest-Rights-Act`** and Biosphere Reserve designation to **`Environment-and-Ecology/basic/07_Biosphere-Reserves-and-Ramsar-Sites`** |
| ⚠️ **Transshipment economics, port-led development, capacity and market share, Sagarmala** | 🔁 **`Geography/basic/33_Transport-Trade-and-Indian-Space-Programme`** and **`Economy/basic/18_Infrastructure-PPPs-Logistics-and-Public-Investment`** |
| ⚠️ **Maritime security, string of pearls, Act East, Indo-Pacific strategy, the Indian Ocean as an intersection zone** | 🔁 **`International-Relations/basic/04_Indo-Pacific-Indian-Ocean-and-Maritime-Security`** |
| ⚠️ **Tsunami, earthquake and cyclone mechanism, warning and preparedness doctrine** | 🔁 **`Geography/basic/03_Vulcanism-and-Earthquakes`** and **`Disaster-Management/basic/06_Tsunami-and-Coastal-Hazard-Management`** |

❗ ⚠️ **The boundary rule applied here, stated so it can be checked.** ⚠️ **Geography teaches the location, the depth, the land-use footprint, the reef and hazard exposure, and the arithmetic.** ⚠️ **Every clearance, committee, tribal-policy, forest-law, port-economic and strategic element is named once and routed by exact path.** ❗ **The 2026 GS-II tribal-development demand is live, recent and routed; any attempt to argue tribal policy inside a Geography edition fails the boundary.**

### 7. 🇮🇳 India hook — the whole lesson, and the discipline it demonstrates

🇮🇳 ⚠️ This lesson is entirely Indian. ❗ Its value is **not** the project; it is the **method**, and the method transfers to every quantitative appraisal a candidate will ever face:

> ⚠️ *"Read a project's own factsheets as sources. Sum what they publish. State which sums close — the phasing to 166.10 sq km, the land classes to 166.10 sq km, the felling to 7.11 lakh trees, and the tribal-reserve net addition to 3.912 sq km on the unrounded value. State which do not — the felling area plus green zone falling 14.90 sq km short of the diverted forest, the two compensatory-afforestation accounts differing by a factor and by 1,430.83 ha, and a township whose stated extent is the entire project. And check every ratio's denominator: 1.82 per cent is of the Andaman and Nicobar Islands' total forest area, not of the island."*

❗ ⚠️ **One final discipline (R-16, U-26).** ⚠️ **A project factsheet is not a measured outcome.** ⚠️ Everything above is a statement of **plan, allocation and condition as of 1 May 2026**. ❗ **A clearance condition is not an observed result**, exactly as C1's date and data control at lines 221-224 requires. ❗ **This edition states the position as of the PIB documents of 1 May 2026 and makes no claim about any later cabinet, appraisal or construction milestone.**

### 8. Comparison, objection and the residual

| ⚠️ Published group | ⚠️ Closes? | ⚠️ Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| ⚠️ Phases → footprint | ⚠️ **Yes, exactly** | ⚠️ Stated with the sum shown |
| ⚠️ Land classes → footprint | ⚠️ **Yes, exactly** | ⚠️ Stated with the sum shown |
| ⚠️ Phase felling → total felling | ⚠️ **Yes, exactly** | ⚠️ Stated with the sum shown |
| ⚠️ Tribal reserve net addition | ⚠️ **Yes, on the unrounded value** | ⚠️ Rounding disclosed (N-36) |
| ⚠️ Felling area + green zone → diverted forest | ❗ **No — 14.90 sq km short** | ❗ Residual stated; unassigned (N-35) |
| ⚠️ Phase-I diversion vs felling area | ❗ **No — 48.65 against 49.86** | ❗ Both stated; neither adjusted |
| ⚠️ Compensatory afforestation, two accounts | ❗ **No — on extent, on State and on the "equivalent" claim** | ❗ Both quoted and dated; no averaging (N-05) |
| ⚠️ Township extent vs project footprint | ❗ **No — they are identical** | ❗ 166.10 sq km cited as the footprint only (N-04) |

**Strongest objection.** ⚠️ Auditing a government factsheet's arithmetic is not geography; it is accountancy, and it also risks reading as advocacy against the project.

**Reply.** ⚠️ It is geography because **every quantity audited is a spatial quantity** — areas, footprints, land-class partitions, reserve overlaps and a forest-cover ratio — and **the central error is a denominator error about which area is being divided by which**, which is precisely a spatial-reasoning error. ❗ On the advocacy point, the method is **symmetrical and is demonstrably so**: this lesson reports **four sums that close exactly** before it reports four that do not, and it declines to assert that any figure is wrong, that any discrepancy is concealment, or that the project is justified or unjustified. ⚠️ **Showing which parts of an official account are internally consistent is as much a part of the audit as showing which are not**, and an audit that only found faults would be the advocacy the objection fears.

❓ **Residual, which this edition does not close.** ⚠️ ❓ **The 14.90 sq km residual is unexplained in the sources and is not assigned here.** ⚠️ ❓ **The compensatory-afforestation divergence is not adjudicated**; neither document is preferred. ⚠️ ❓ **The ISFR recorded forest area for Andaman and Nicobar was not retrieved (U-03)**, so the ~7,184 sq km denominator is reported as **implied by division only**. ⚠️ ❓ **Great Nicobar's island area is not stated (U-06)**, so the *"order of 12 per cent"* comparison is given as an order of magnitude and not as a computed figure. ⚠️ ❓ **The project's status after 1 May 2026 was not verified (U-26)**.

### 9. Traps specific to this lesson

- ❌ ⚠️ **Writing "1.82 per cent of the island's forest cover".** ❗ It is **of the total forest area of the Andaman and Nicobar Islands**; the implied denominator is about **7,184 sq km**, and **the denominator must be named** (N-03).
- ❌ ⚠️ **Repeating "16,610 hectares" as a township area.** ❗ **It is 166.10 sq km — the entire project footprint** (N-04).
- ❌ ⚠️ **Averaging or repairing the compensatory-afforestation figures.** ❗ **Quote both, date both, state the divergence** (N-05).
- ❌ ⚠️ **Presenting the felling-and-green-zone partition as closing.** ❗ **49.86 + 65.99 = 115.85 against 130.75; residual 14.90 sq km** (N-35).
- ❌ ⚠️ **Using 73.07 in a subtraction without disclosing the rounding.** ❗ **84.10 − 11.032 = 73.068**, and the **+3.912** net addition closes only on the unrounded value (N-36).
- ❌ ⚠️ **Computing Great Nicobar's area from "50 × 25 km".** ❗ Only ICRZ 2019's *">1000 sq.km"* may be stated (N-29, U-06).
- ❌ ⚠️ **Treating the factsheet as a measured outcome.** ❗ It is a statement of plan and condition **as of 1 May 2026** (U-26).
- ❌ ⚠️ **Arguing tribal policy, EIA procedure, transshipment economics or Indo-Pacific strategy.** 🔁 Four separate owners, named above.
- ❌ ⚠️ **Calling the governing instrument "CRZ".** ❗ **ICRZ 2019**, and Great Nicobar is a **Group-I** island under it (N-01).

### UPSC application

- **Syllabus:** ⚠️ Mapped clause, **second limb** — *changes in critical geographical features*. ⚠️ A 166.10 sq km land-use change on a Group-I island, of which 130.75 sq km is forest, is that limb in its most literal form.
- **Verified PYQ:** ⚠️ **No owned demand concerns this project.** 🔁 ❗ **Four adjacent demands converge here and all four are routed by exact path**: 2026 GS-II Q6 (tribal development), 2026 GS-I Q16 (Atlantic-to-Indo-Pacific trade shift), 2026 Prelims Q35 (Sagarmala) and 2022 Prelims Q49 (biorock). ❗ **No key, option, elimination or answer approach is offered for any objective demand anywhere in this file.**
- **Traps:** ⚠️ The nine above. ❗ The denominator trap is the single highest-value item in the topic; the township trap is the most easily checked.
- **Answer use:** ❗ Write the **appraisal as geography plus arithmetic, and route the rest in one clause**. ⚠️ *"Galathea Bay offers over 20 metres of natural depth about 40 nautical miles from the East-West shipping route on an ICRZ Group-I island; the project's footprint of 166.10 sq km — 35.35 revenue and 130.75 forest land, phased to 2047 — is stated as approximately 2 per cent of the Andaman and Nicobar Islands' area and the forest diversion as 1.82 per cent of the Union Territory's total forest area, not of the island; its phasing, land-class and felling sums close exactly while its felling-and-green-zone partition leaves 14.90 sq km unaccounted and its two compensatory-afforestation accounts, published on the same day, do not agree. Clearance conditions, tribal policy, port economics and maritime strategy belong to their respective owners."*

Revision notes:

- 📰 **Locational facts (PIB backgrounder, 1 May 2026):** about **40 nautical miles** from the **East-West international shipping route**; **over 20 m** natural depth at **Galathea Bay**; **ICTT 14.2 million TEU**; airport at **4,000 Peak Hour Passengers** against Sri Vijaya Puram's *"approximately 1.8 million passengers annually"*, opening at *"at least 1 million"* and growing to *"approximately 10 million"*; **450 MVA** gas and solar replacing diesel.
- ✅ **Khullar, printed 89:** Great Nicobar is **147 km from Sumatra**; ⚠️ **Indira Point** is India's **southernmost territory** — ❗ **not Indira Col**.
- 📰 **ICRZ 2019:** **Group-I**, *">1000 sq.km"* → **ICRZ HTL to 200 m**, **NDZ 100 m**; ❗ **the NDZ does not apply within notified Port limits**.
- ✅ ❗ **SUMS THAT CLOSE:** **72.12 + 45.27 + 48.71 = 166.10** · **35.35 + 130.75 = 166.10** · **2.79 + 3.41 + 0.91 = 7.11 lakh** · **84.10 − 11.032 = 73.068**, and **76.98 − 73.068 = +3.912**.
- 📰 **7.11 lakh of an estimated 18.65 lakh standing trees = 38.1 per cent.** 📰 **Phase I involves 40.01 sq km of tribal area.** 📰 **Tribal reserve 751.070 sq km**; settlements **New Chingen** and **Rajiv Nagar**; **Shompen about 237**, **Nicobarese about 1,094**.
- 📰 **166.1 sq km = *"approximately 2 per cent of the total area of Andaman and Nicobar Islands"***; ⚠️ re-computed against 8,249 sq km = **2.014 per cent**. ❗ **A percentage of an archipelago total is itself a counting-convention claim (Lesson 13).**
- ❌ **[N-36]** **73.07 is the rounded form of 73.068**; the net addition closes **only on the unrounded value**. ❗ Disclose the rounding; never "fix" the published figures.
- ❌ ❗ **SUMS THAT DO NOT CLOSE:**
  - **[N-35]** **49.86 felling + 65.99 green zone = 115.85** against **130.75 diverted forest** → **14.90 sq km residual, unassigned**.
  - **[N-35]** Phase-I diversion **48.65** beside felling area **49.86** — unreconciled.
  - **[N-05]** Backgrounder **97.30 sq km in Haryana** (ratio exactly **2.0** to 48.65) against FAQ **24,750.93 ha** total with *"about **17,000 ha** in Haryana"* and **6,320.10 ha in Madhya Pradesh** → **23,320.10**, short by **1,430.83 ha**; and **97.30 sq km = 9,730 ha**, not 17,000; and **1,414.95 ha** called *"equivalent to the diverted area"* against **13,075 ha** — a factor of **9.24**. ❗ **Quote both. Date both. Do not average, repair or choose.**
  - **[N-04]** *"A new township spanning **16,610 hectares**"* **= 166.10 sq km = the whole footprint**. ❗ **Cite 166.10 sq km as the project area; never repeat 16,610 ha as a township area.**
- ❌ ❗ **[N-03] THE DENOMINATOR TRAP:** backgrounder *"1.82% of **island forest cover**"*; FAQ, same day, *"1.82% of the **total forest area of A & N Islands**"*. ⚠️ Implied denominator **130.75 ÷ 0.0182 ≈ 7,184 sq km**. ❗ **[U-03] The ISFR figure was NOT retrieved; ~7,184 is *implied by division*, never cited.** ⚠️ Against the island itself the diversion is **of the order of 12 per cent**. ❗ **Every use of 1.82 per cent names its denominator.**
- 📰 **Legal basis for off-island afforestation, stated once:** **MoEF&CC Guidelines dated 22.05.2019** under the **Forest (Conservation) Act, 1980**, exempting States and UTs with **over 75 per cent forest cover** from providing non-forest land locally.
- 📰 **Named once, then routed:** Prior EC under the **EIA Notification 2006** and **ICRZ 2019** with **42 conditions**; appraisers **ZSI, SACON, WII, IISc**, plus **IITs, NIOT, NCCR, NIO**; **three Monitoring Committees** plus an **Overarching Committee** chaired by the **Chief Secretary**; *"coral protection and translocation"*; **NOC from the Ministry of Tribal Affairs**; **Empowered Committee**; **NGT order of 3 April 2023**; *"seismically sensitive and cyclone-prone"*.
- 🔁 **Four routes:** tribal policy → `Social-Justice/basic/08` and `Polity/basic/Scheduled-and-Tribal-Areas` · clearance, ecology, forest law and restoration → `Environment-and-Ecology/basic/24`, `basic/11`, `basic/07` · port and transshipment economics → `Geography/basic/33` and `Economy/basic/18` · maritime strategy → `International-Relations/basic/04`.
- ❗ **[U-26 / R-16] A project factsheet is NOT a measured outcome.** ❗ Position stated **as of 1 May 2026**; no claim about any later milestone.
- 🔑 **Mnemonic — "166.10 closes, 115.85 does not, and 1.82 per cent is of the Andaman and Nicobar total forest area, not of the island."**

### Concept check

**Question:** A candidate writes: "The Great Nicobar project will divert only 1.82% of the island's forest cover, affecting 16,610 hectares of township and compensated by 97.30 sq km of afforestation in Haryana." Audit this sentence completely — including the **denominator** the percentage actually uses and whether **16,610 hectares** can be a component of a **166.10 sq km** footprint — and state the correct formulation.

**Model answer:** Three defects, all of them quantitative, and two of them are among the most consequential errors available in this topic. **First, the denominator is wrong.** The backgrounder's Key Takeaways do say *"diversion of 1.82% of island forest cover"*, but the FAQ published by the same body on the same day, 1 May 2026, states what is meant: *"130.75 sq. km of forest area is proposed to be diverted for the project which is only approximately 1.82% of the total forest area of A & N Islands."* The denominator is the **Union Territory's total forest area**, implied by division as roughly 7,184 sq km — a figure that must itself be labelled as implied, since the India State of Forest Report value was not retrieved. Measured against Great Nicobar island, the diversion is of the **order of 12 per cent**, and no more precise statement is possible because the island's area is not independently available. **Second, 16,610 hectares is not the township.** It equals **166.10 sq km exactly**, which the same document gives as the **total project area**. A component cannot also be the whole, so the correct citation is 166.10 sq km as the project footprint, composed of 35.35 sq km of revenue land and 130.75 sq km of forest land, phased as 72.12, 45.27 and 48.71 sq km to 2047 — all three sums closing exactly. **Third, the afforestation figure is one of two incompatible official accounts.** The backgrounder gives 97.30 sq km in Haryana against a Phase-I diversion of 48.65 sq km, a ratio of exactly 2.0; the FAQ gives a total of 24,750.93 ha, of which about 17,000 ha in Haryana and 6,320.10 ha in Madhya Pradesh. But 97.30 sq km is 9,730 ha, not 17,000; the two states sum to 23,320.10 ha, leaving 1,430.83 ha unaccounted; and the 1,414.95 ha described as *"equivalent to the diverted area"* is 9.24 times smaller than the 13,075 ha actually diverted. The correct formulation quotes both accounts with their shared date, states the divergence, and neither averages nor chooses. It also notes that the felling area of 49.86 sq km plus the green zone of 65.99 sq km leaves a **14.90 sq km residual** against the 130.75 sq km diverted.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not treat a percentage as self-explanatory because it is official. A ratio is a statement about two quantities, and an answer that reproduces the numerator without the denominator has reproduced half a fact. In this case the same organisation published both the ambiguous and the explicit form on the same day, so the correct denominator is available in the project's own record and there is no excuse for the wrong one.

**Original Mains practice [20 marks]:** Appraise the Great Nicobar project as a problem in geography rather than in policy, using its own published figures. *(Appraise — answer in about 250 words.)*

**Model (about 250 words):** Geographically the project is a locational and land-use question. Its site qualities are physical: **over 20 metres** of natural depth at Galathea Bay and a position about **40 nautical miles** from the East-West international shipping route, on an island classified **Group-I** under ICRZ 2019 because its geographical area exceeds 1,000 sq km, and therefore carrying a 200-metre regulated zone and a 100-metre no-development zone from which notified port limits are exempt. The land-use change is **166.10 sq km**, composed of **35.35 sq km revenue** and **130.75 sq km forest** land, phased **72.12**, **45.27** and **48.71** sq km across 2025-47 — sums that close exactly, as does felling of **2.79 + 3.41 + 0.91 = 7.11 lakh** trees, about 38.1 per cent of an estimated 18.65 lakh standing. The tribal-reserve arithmetic also closes: 84.10 sq km of overlap less 11.032 settled since 1972 gives 73.068 de-notified, against 76.98 re-notified, a net addition of **3.912 sq km** on the unrounded value. Other published sums do not close. Felling area **49.86** plus green zone **65.99** gives 115.85 against 130.75 diverted, a **14.90 sq km** residual; and two compensatory-afforestation accounts published on **1 May 2026** differ in extent, in State and in their *"equivalent to the diverted area"* claim. The most consequential figure needs its denominator: **1.82 per cent** refers to the total forest area of the Andaman and Nicobar Islands, not of the island. Clearance, rights, economics and strategy belong to other owners.

**Rubric (quantified, 20 marks):** **5** for the locational appraisal confined to physical and positional facts — depth, distance to the shipping route, Group-I classification and the setback consequence — without drifting into capacity or strategy; **5** for the footprint arithmetic that closes, with at least three of the four groups re-summed and shown to close, including the rounding disclosure on the tribal-reserve figures; **5** for the arithmetic that does not close, with the 14.90 sq km residual and the two afforestation accounts both stated, dated to the same day, and neither averaged nor adjudicated; **3** for the denominator trap handled correctly, naming the Andaman and Nicobar total forest area, labelling the implied denominator as implied, and giving the island-level comparison only as an order of magnitude; **2** for routing discipline, with at least three of the four external owners named by exact path and no policy argument attempted. Deduct 4 for writing 1.82 per cent without its denominator; deduct 2 for repeating 16,610 hectares as a township area when it equals the 166.10 sq km footprint.

⚠️ *The spine is an answer-construction device, not a map; the drill is a recall checklist whose reference is **M1 to M6** inside this edition. ❗ **No previous-year objective question is used as a key for any item on the drill.***

### 1. The intuition, before any terminology

⚠️ Sixteen lessons have produced a great deal of material. ❗ **The owned Mains demand allows 150 words.**

⚠️ That is the whole problem of this lesson, and it is a real one rather than a presentational one. ⚠️ A candidate who has learned this topic properly arrives at the 2019 question holding a seven-axis mechanism table, three official reef-area series, an inter-ministerial contradiction, a sediment-starvation chain, a freshwater-lens chain, two theories of reef origin and a complete island inventory — **and can use about one-twentieth of it**.

❗ **The skill being taught is therefore selection under a budget**, and the budget is not negotiable: **10 marks, 150 words, directive *Assess*, instruction *with examples*.**

### 2. The two owned demands, stated exactly and treated differently

| ⚠️ | ⚠️ **O1** | ⚠️ **O2** |
|---|---|---|
| ⚠️ Year and paper | **2018 Prelims GS-I, Question 87** | **2019 Mains GS-I, Question 4** |
| ⚠️ Ledger demand, verbatim | *"Coral reef global distribution tropical waters and biodiversity"* | *"Global warming impact on the coral life system"* |
| ⚠️ Directive and length | ⚠️ Objective question | **"Assess with examples · 10 marks · 150 words"** |
| ⚠️ Ledger status | ❗ *"Routed; key unavailable locally"* | ⚠️ *"Routed to owning topic"* |
| ⚠️ Treatment in this edition | ❗ **Demand statement and capability statement only.** ❗ **No option. No key. No elimination. No convergence.** | ⚠️ **Full model answer at 150 words, built below** |

❗ ⚠️ **The answer-neutrality rule, restated at the point where it would be easiest to break.** ❗ **O1 has no locally available key — the ledger says so in terms — and none is inferred, reconstructed or implied anywhere in this file.** ❗ **Original Mains questions authored by this edition are accompanied by model answers; objective previous-year demands are never accompanied by answers.** ❗ **The edition may answer what it writes and may never answer what the Commission wrote in objective form.**

⚠️ **What O1 is used for, and it is used properly.** ⚠️ The **capability** tested is the global distribution of reefs in tropical waters together with their biodiversity significance. ⚠️ **Lesson 5 supplies the distribution and its four mechanisms; Lesson 3 supplies the biodiversity premise as constructed habitat; Lesson 4 supplies the controls behind both.** ❗ A candidate who has those three lessons can meet the capability without anyone having told them an answer.

❗ ⚠️ **And the ownership profile, stated plainly and without decoration.** ⚠️ **One owned Prelims demand and one owned Mains demand, from 2018 and 2019.** ❗ **Nothing is owned in 2020, 2021, 2022, 2023, 2024, 2025 or 2026.** ❗ **No recency is manufactured by claiming adjacent questions.**

### 3. The directive-decoding table, from the owner

✅ **C1 §10.1** supplies the decoding table, which is reproduced here because it is the owner's own instrument and because the *"Do not"* column is where marks are lost:

| ✅ If the question says | ✅ It is really asking for | ❗ Do **not** |
|---|---|---|
| ✅ *"Discuss the conditions necessary for coral growth"* | ✅ Temperature, depth and light, salinity, clear sediment-free water, firm substratum and wave-agitated oxygenation — **each with the reason** | ❗ List conditions without explaining why each matters |
| ✅ *"Explain Darwin's subsidence theory"* | ✅ Fringing → barrier → atoll as **one sequence** driven by island subsidence with upward reef growth, plus the alternative glacio-eustatic explanation | ❗ Present the sequence as three unrelated reef types |
| ✅ *"Distinguish bleaching from acidification"* | ✅ The seven-axis table — different driver, chemistry, timescale, reversibility and leverage | ❗ Treat them as the same warming problem |
| ✅ *"Assess the threats to island nations"* | ✅ Sediment supply, freshwater lens, compound flooding and adaptive island dynamics, **alongside** sea-level rise | ❗ **Reduce it to inundation** |

⚠️ **And the directive this edition's owned demand actually carries — *Assess* — decoded in the same form:**

| ⚠️ *Assess* requires | ⚠️ Which means, concretely |
|---|---|
| ⚠️ **A judgement, not a description** | ⚠️ The answer must say **how serious, in what respect, and with what limit** — not merely what happens |
| ⚠️ **Evidence cited for the judgement** | ⚠️ *"With examples"* makes this explicit and non-optional |
| ⚠️ **A qualification** | ⚠️ An assessment with no limit is an assertion |
| ⚠️ **Proportionality to the budget** | ⚠️ At 150 words, **one mechanism, two examples, one qualification** |

### 4. The reusable seven-step spine, and how it compresses

✅ **C1 §10.2** gives a reusable 10-mark spine for reef vulnerability in seven steps: **thesis** (reefs are threatened less by any single event than by the collapse of the balance between calcification and erosion); **baseline** (the narrow environmental window); **global stressors** (bleaching and acidification, properly distinguished); **local stressors**; **consequence** (fisheries, wave attenuation, tourism, island sediment supply); **balance** (local management improves recovery capacity but cannot address acidification); and a **graded conclusion**.

❗ ⚠️ **Seven steps will not fit in 150 words, and pretending otherwise is the error this lesson exists to prevent.** ⚠️ The compression, shown explicitly:

| ✅ C1's seven steps | ⚠️ At 250+ words | ⚠️ At 150 words |
|---|---|---|
| ✅ 1 Thesis | ⚠️ A full sentence on the calcification-erosion balance | ⚠️ **Merged into Move 1** as the two-pathway distinction |
| ✅ 2 Baseline — the window | ⚠️ One sentence | ⚠️ **Cut.** Implied by the mechanism |
| ✅ 3 Global stressors | ⚠️ Both, with their axes | ⚠️ **One stated in full (heat), one named (carbon)** |
| ✅ 4 Local stressors | ⚠️ The full list | ⚠️ **Compressed to a half-clause in the qualification** |
| ✅ 5 Consequence | ⚠️ Four consequences | ⚠️ **Cut**, or one clause if space remains |
| ✅ 6 Balance | ⚠️ A full sentence | ⚠️ **Becomes Move 5, the qualification** |
| ✅ 7 Graded conclusion | ⚠️ A sentence | ⚠️ **Merged into Move 4**, the compounding node |

❗ **The rule: at 150 words, steps 1 and 7 merge into the opening and closing moves, step 4 shrinks to a clause, and steps 2 and 5 are cut.**

### 5. 🇮🇳 India hook — the model answer for O2, built and then shown

🇮🇳 ⚠️ **The owned demand, as the ledger records it:** 2019 Mains GS-I Question 4, *"Global warming impact on the coral life system"*, directive **"Assess with examples"**, **10 marks / 150 words**, routed to `Geography/basic/11_Islands-and-Coral-Reefs`.

❗ ⚠️ **The model below is this edition's own answer to a Mains question, which the answer-neutrality rule permits. It answers no objective demand.**

**Model answer for the owned 2019 GS-I demand (about 150 words):** Global warming reaches the coral life system by two distinct pathways, and assessing it requires separating them. Thermally, sustained sea-surface temperature above the local tolerance breaks the coral-zooxanthellae symbiosis: the polyp expels the algae that subsidise its calcification, loses colour and its main nutrition, and starves — though a bleached coral is not a dead coral, and recovery follows if the stress ends quickly. Chemically, carbon-dioxide uptake lowers carbonate-ion availability, so rebuilding becomes slower and costlier. India's record shows both the impact and the difficulty of assessing it: the Ministry of Earth Sciences recorded Lakshadweep at 84.6 per cent bleaching for 2023-24 on 04 DEC 2024, and the environment ministry reported about 98 per cent on Kadmat, Kavaratti and Kiltan during the Fourth Global Coral Bleaching Event on 16.12.2024. The decisive effect is reduced recovery capacity between events, not a single mortality episode.

⚠️ **Why each move is where it is, stated so the construction can be copied:**

- ⚠️ **Move 1** establishes that the question has **two answers, not one**, which is the assessment move; without it the answer cannot be more than descriptive.
- ⚠️ **Move 2** states **one** mechanism fully. ❗ Two full mechanisms would consume the entire budget.
- ⚠️ **Move 3** supplies **two dated, attributed, official Indian examples** — satisfying *"with examples"* precisely, and demonstrating date discipline in the same breath.
- ⚠️ **Move 4** is the **compounding node**, which is the single highest-value analytical sentence in the topic and the reason this is an *assess* answer rather than a *describe* answer.
- ⚠️ **Move 5** is the **qualification**, carrying both the recovery fact and its limit — **no recovery percentage exists** — in one clause.

⚠️ **And what was deliberately left out**, which a candidate should be able to name: the full seven-axis table, the three reef-area series, the inter-ministerial contradiction in its full form, the sediment-starvation and freshwater-lens chains, the local-stressor list, reef services, reef morphology, Darwin and Daly, the island inventory, and ICRZ 2019.

🔁 ❗ **One item is excluded not for space but by ownership, and it is excluded here as the last route-out of the edition.** ❗ **India's coastline length does not appear in any form in this edition.** ⚠️ MoEFCC's own 2019 reef answer opens with *"India has a coastline of nearly 8000 km"*, which is a **fifth** official coastline figure beside the series Topic 10 carries. 🔁 **The entire coastline-measurement series routes to `Geography/basic/10_Coastal-Landforms`**, and the reason it is named here, in the capstone, is that the temptation to use it as a scene-setting opening is highest in exactly the kind of answer this lesson builds.

### 6. The two evidence units, carried as written

✅ **C1 §10.3** supplies two claim-evidence-significance-limitation units. ❗ **Both are reproduced with their limitation clauses intact, because the limitation is what makes a unit usable in an *assess* answer.**

> ✅ **Claim:** coral reefs are a coastal-protection asset, not only an ecological one.
> ✅ **Evidence:** a reef crest dissipates wave energy before it reaches the shore, which is why reef-fringed coasts and the islands behind them experience attenuated wave attack.
> ✅ **Significance:** it converts reef loss into a direct coastal-hazard and infrastructure question.
> ✅ **Limitation:** reefs attenuate ordinary wave energy far better than extreme surge, so they supplement rather than replace other protection.

> ✅ **Claim:** an island can be threatened by chemistry as much as by water level.
> ✅ **Evidence:** low reef islands are built from carbonate produced by the living reef, and their freshwater is a thin lens vulnerable to overwash and abstraction.
> ✅ **Significance:** it explains why water security and sediment supply fail before inundation does.
> ✅ **Limitation:** reef islands can also accrete and migrate, so their fate is not uniformly determined.

⚠️ **How to deploy a unit under a budget:** at 250 words, write all four parts. ⚠️ At 150 words, write **claim and limitation** and let the evidence be implicit in the example already cited. ❗ **Never write the claim without the limitation**, because a claim without a limit is the commonest reason an *assess* answer is marked as a *describe* answer.

### 7. The map consolidation register

❗ ⚠️ **Six map assets are carried inside this edition because the repository's map bank was measured against 74 island and reef assets and found to hold 34, with 40 absent.** ❗ **The bank is not owned by this lane, was not edited, and the gap is closed internally.**

|---|---|---|---|
